4.1
Limits from rare kaon decays

4.1.1
K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu}

The decay
K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu}
is one of the golden modes for testing the SM, since
long-distance contributions are negligibly small.
The effective Lagrangian comes from a combination of
Z
-penguin and box-diagram and it
is given by
Buchalla:1995vs
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\alpha(M_{Z})}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{\text{W}}}\sum_{\ell=e,\mu,\tau}[V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd}X^{\ell}(x_{c})+V_{ts}^{*}V_{td}X^{\ell}(x_{t})](\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})(\overline{\nu_{\ell L}}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{\ell L})+\text{h.c.}

(243)

X(x_{a})
are the Inami-Lim function including QCD and electroweak corrections,
with
x_{a}=m^{2}_{a}/M^{2}_{W}
,
a=c,t
.
The index
\ell
denotes the lepton flavor.
The dependence on the charged lepton mass
is negligible for the top contribution due to
m_{t}\gg m_{\ell}
,

X^{\ell}(x_{t})=X(x_{t})
,
whereas, being
m_{\tau}
comparable with
m_{c}
,
for the c-quark contribution
the box with
\ell=\tau
gives somewhat different contribution
from
\ell=e,\mu

Buchalla:1995vs
,

X^{e}(x_{c})=X^{e}(x_{c})\neq X^{\tau}(x_{c})
. Therefore, it is used an averaged value

\bar{X}=\frac{1}{3}\left(X^{e}(x_{c})+X^{\mu}(x_{c})+X^{\tau}(x_{c})\right)
.
Then the effective Lagrangian (
243
) can also be written as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{F}_{K}(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})\sum_{e,\mu,\tau}(\overline{\nu_{\ell L}}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{\ell L})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha(M_{Z})}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{\text{W}}}\Big(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td}X(x_{t})+(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})\bar{X}(x_{c})\Big)=

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha(M_{Z})}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{\text{W}}}\lambda^{5}\Big(A^{2}(1-\rho-i\eta)X(x_{t})+(1-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2})P_{c}(X)\Big)

(244)

where
P_{c}(X)=\frac{1}{\lambda^{4}}\bar{X}
, and
we used the Wolfenstein parameterization:

\displaystyle\text{Re}(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})=-\lambda(1-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2})\,,

\displaystyle\text{Re}(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})=-(1-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2})A^{2}\lambda^{5}(1-\rho)\,,

\displaystyle\text{Im}(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})=A^{2}\lambda^{5}\eta\,.

(245)

The top contribution gives
X(x_{t})=1.481

Buras15
(central value).
For the charm contribution,
using the values in table
4
, from the formula in ref.
Brod1

we obtain
P_{c}(X)\approx 0.351
.
By using
\rho=0.126
,
\eta=0.392
and
the central values in table
4
we have

\mathcal{F}_{K}\approx(-3.7+i\,1.2)\times 10^{-6}
,

|\mathcal{F}_{K}|\approx 3.9\times 10^{-6}
.
The predicted SM contribution for the branching ratio can be written as:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}\approx k_{K^{+}}|\mathcal{F}_{K}|^{2}\>,

\displaystyle k_{K^{+}}=\frac{G_{F}^{2}\tau_{K^{+}}M^{5}_{K^{+}}f^{2}_{+}(0)\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{+}}{64\pi^{3}}\approx 5.43

(246)

where
f_{+}(0)=0.9699(15)
is the form factor,

\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{+}=0.15269
is the phase space integral
Mescia:2007kn

and we have used the values in table
4
for the kaon mass and mean life,.
In this way, we obtain our benchmark value

\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}\approx 0.82\cdot 10^{-10}
,
which is within the range of the estimate reported by PDG
Br
(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}=(0.85\pm 0.05)\cdot 10^{-10}

PDG18
.
The SM expectation is compatible with
the experimental branching ratio
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{exp}}=(1.7\pm 1.1)\cdot 10^{-10}

(247)

With future experimental precision,
any deviation from the SM prediction of this golden mode branching ratio
would indicate towards new physics.

Figure 6:
Tree level contribution to the rare kaon decays

K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu}
,
K^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu}
arising from the mixing of the SM families with the extra downtype vectorlike quark.

In our BSM scenario with extra vectorlike
b^{\prime}
quark,
the non-diagonal couplings of
Z
-boson with light quarks
in eqs. (
4
)
induce at tree level the operator with the same structure as the SM one (
243
),
as shown in the diagram in figure
6
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{g^{2}}{M^{2}_{Z}\cos^{2}\theta_{W}}\left(\frac{1}{2}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}\right)(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})\frac{1}{2}\sum_{e,\mu,\tau}(\overline{\nu_{\ell\text{L}}}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{\ell\text{L}})=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{2}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})\sum_{e,\mu,\tau}(\overline{\nu_{\ell\text{L}}}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{\ell\text{L}})

(248)

Thus,
the new operator (
4.1.1
) contributes to the decay

K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu}
, in interference with the SM (
243
).
The total branching ratio becomes:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{tot}}\approx\,k_{K^{+}}\left|\frac{1}{2}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}+\mathcal{F}_{K}\right|^{2}=

\displaystyle=\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}\left|\frac{\frac{1}{2}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}}{\mathcal{F}_{K}}+1\right|^{2}

(249)

The experimental result (
247
) implies, at
95\%
C.L., the upper limit:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{tot}}<3.9\cdot 10^{-10}

(250)

Then we have:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\frac{1}{2}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}}{\mathcal{F}_{K}}+1\right|<2.2

(251)

figure
13
shows the constraint (
251
) in terms of the modulus and
phase of the elements
V_{L4d}
,
V_{L4s}
,
using
V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}=|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|e^{i\delta^{d}_{L21}}
,

\delta^{d}_{L21}=\delta^{d}_{L2}-\delta^{d}_{L1}
, from eqs. (
4
) and (
211
).
Depending on the unknown relative phase, the limit results in the constraint on the modulus:

\displaystyle|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|<(0.9\div 2.5)\cdot 10^{-5}

(252)

As can be seen,
in the most conservative case of destructive interference, the new amplitude can be up to
three times the SM amplitude:

\displaystyle\frac{|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|}{2|\mathcal{F}_{K}|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta_{K^{+}}=3.2

(253)

In this case we can express the experimental limit in terms of the ratio
\Delta_{K^{+}}
:

\displaystyle|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2.5\cdot 10^{-5}\left[\frac{\Delta_{K^{+}}}{3.2}\right]\left[\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{K}|}{3.9\cdot 10^{-6}}\right]

(254)

4.1.2
K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu}

The second golden mode is the
decay
K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu}
.
In the Standard Model it is described by the same Lagrangian as in
(
4.1.1
). However this decay
proceeds almost entirely through direct CP violation,
hence it is
completely dominated by short-distance loop diagrams with top quark exchanges and the charm contribution can be neglected
Buras:1998raa
.
In fact,
with the phase convention

C|K^{0}\rangle=|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle
(or
CP|K^{0}\rangle=-|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle
)
we have:

\displaystyle\langle\pi^{0}|(\bar{d}s)_{\text{V$-$A}}|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle=-\langle\pi^{0}|(\bar{s}d)_{\text{V$-$A}}|K^{0}\rangle

(255)

\displaystyle K_{\text{L}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[(1+\epsilon)K^{0}+(1-\epsilon)\bar{K}^{0}]

(256)

However, the terms of indirect (
\Delta S=2
) CP violation (proportional to
\epsilon
)
can be neglected in this decay and the dominant contribution comes from the imaginary part
of the operator (
4.1.1
):

\displaystyle\langle\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu}|\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM}}|\frac{K^{0}+\bar{K}^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{i\,2\text{Im}\mathcal{F}_{K}}{\sqrt{2}}\langle\pi^{0}|(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})|K^{0}\rangle\sum_{\ell=e,\mu,\tau}\langle\overline{\nu_{\ell\text{L}}}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{\ell\text{L}}\rangle

(257)

The SM predicted branching ratio can be written as:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{L}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}\approx k_{K_{L}}|\mathcal{F}_{K}|^{2}\>,

\displaystyle k_{K_{L}}=\frac{G_{F}^{2}\tau_{K_{L}}M^{5}_{K^{0}}f^{2}_{+}(0)\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{L}}{64\pi^{3}}

(258)

where
\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{+}=0.16043(31)
is the phase space integral
Mescia:2007kn
.
Putting the values of kaon mass and lifetime (see table
4
)
we obtain
\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}\approx 3.5\times 10^{-11}
,
which practically agrees with the estimate reported by PDG
\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{SM}}=(3.0\pm 0.2)\times 10^{-11}

PDG18
.

On the other hand, the experimental limit on this decay is
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{exp}}<3.0\times 10^{-9}\quad(90\%\,\text{C.L.})

(259)

which is two orders of magnitude larger than the SM expectation. Therefore, there is still much room for new physics.

In our BSM scenario with extra vectorlike
b^{\prime}
quark,
the new contribution comes from the imaginary part of
the Lagrangian in eq. (
4.1.1
) Since the interference term with the SM contribution can be neglected,
the experimental limit can be directly applied to the new contribution:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu})_{\text{new}}\approx k_{K_{L}}\frac{1}{4}\big(\text{Im}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})\big)^{2}<\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}

(260)

So in the given parameterization (
4
), (
211
) we have:

\displaystyle|\text{Im}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})|=|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}||\sin(\delta^{d}_{L21})|<2.2\times 10^{-5}\left[\frac{\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}}{3.0\times 10^{-9}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(261)

This condition is shown in figure
13
.
Then, the present experimental limit allows
the new contribution to be one order of magnitude larger than the SM contribution:

\displaystyle\frac{|\text{Im}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})|}{2|\text{Im}\mathcal{F}_{K}|}<9.3\left[\frac{\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}}{3.0\cdot 10^{-9}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(262)

So the discovery of the decay
K_{L}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\nu\bar{\nu}

with branching ratio larger than the SM expectation can be a signal for BSM physics.

4.1.3
K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-}

The decay
K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-}
contains a direct CP violating contribution,
indirect CP violating contribution, interference between them,
and also a small CP conserving contribution
PDG18
.
The CP conserving contribution to the amplitude is dominated by a two photon exchange

K_{L}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\gamma\gamma\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-}
,
with both an absorptive and a dispersive part.
Using the the decay
K_{L}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\gamma\gamma
,
it is estimated that the CP-conserving branching ratio is of order
\sim O(10^{-13})

PDG18
.
The indirectly CP violating amplitude also derives from the coupling of leptons to photons,
it is given by the long-distance dominated
K_{S}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-}

amplitude times the CP parameter
\epsilon_{K}

Buchalla:1995vs
.
The complete CP-violating contribution to the rate assuming a positive sign for the interference term
is estimated to be

\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-})_{\text{CPV}}\approx(3.1\pm 0.9)\times 10^{-11}
, where
the three contributions from indirect, interference and direct CP violation are

(1.76,\,0.9,\,0.45)\times 10^{-11}
respectively
PDG18
.

Only the direct CP-violating amplitude can be calculated in detail within the SM, since
it is short distance dominated.
The relevant operator contains the vector part of the hadronic current and both
axial and vector component of the leptonic current, and
it is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM,SD}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{G_{F}\alpha(M_{Z})}{\sqrt{2}\,2\pi}V_{ts}^{*}V_{td}(\overline{s}\gamma_{\mu}d)\left(\tilde{y}_{V}(\overline{e}\gamma^{\mu}e)+\tilde{y}_{A}(\overline{e}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}e)\right)+\text{h.c.}

(263)

where
\tilde{y}_{V,A}
are a combination of
Inami-Lim functions of box and
Z
and
\gamma
penguin diagrams, as
defined in ref.
Buras:1997fb
.
The direct CP-violating branching ratio in the SM can be written as:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-})_{\text{SM,SD}}=k_{Ke}\left(\frac{G_{F}\alpha(M_{Z})}{\sqrt{2}\,2\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2\text{Im}V_{ts}^{*}V_{td}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^{2}(\tilde{y}_{A}^{2}+\tilde{y}_{V}^{2})

(264)

\displaystyle k_{Ke}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{M^{5}_{K^{0}}\tau_{K_{L}}f^{2}_{+}(0)\mathcal{I}_{e}}{192\pi^{3}}

(265)

where
\mathcal{I}_{e}=0.16043(31)

Mescia:2007kn
is the phase space integral.

Figure 7:
Tree level contribution to the rare kaon decays

K^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
,
K^{0}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
(
\ell=e,\mu
) arising from the mixing of the SM families with the extra downtype vector-like quark.

The new effective Lagrangian contributing to this decay (as shown in figure
7
) is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}(\overline{s}\gamma^{\mu}d)\left[g_{V}\overline{e}\gamma_{\mu}e+g_{A}\overline{e}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}e\right]+\text{h.c.}

(266)

where
g_{A}=\frac{1}{2}
,
g_{V}=-\frac{1}{2}+2\sin^{2}\theta_{\text{W}}\approx-0.038
.
The experimental limit on this decay is
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-})_{\text{exp}}<2.8\times 10^{-10}\quad(90\%\,\text{C.L.})

(267)

which is one order of magnitude bigger than the SM expectation.
Then, we can consider the SM contribution as negligible and
let the new contribution to be the dominant one,
imposing a limit directly on the new contribution:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}e^{+}e^{-})_{\text{new}}=k_{Ke}G_{F}^{2}\,[\text{Im}(V_{L4s}V_{L4d}^{*})]^{2}\left[g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2}\right]<\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}

(268)

In the given parameterization (
4
), (
211
) then:

\displaystyle|\text{Im}(V_{L4s}V_{L4d}^{*})|=|V_{L4s}V_{L4d}^{*}||\sin(\delta^{d}_{L12})|<1.7\times 10^{-5}\left[\frac{\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}}{2.8\cdot 10^{-10}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(269)

where the central values of all the quantities have been used.
Also the decay
K_{L}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
gives a comparable constraint,
being the experimental limit

\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}<3.8\times 10^{-10}\quad(90\%\,\text{C.L.})
,
to be compared with the SM expectation

\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})=(1.5\pm 0.3)\times 10^{-11}
(assuming positive interference between the direct- and indirect-CP violating components)
PDG18
.

4.1.4
K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}

The rare decay
K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
is a CP conserving decay.
Its short-distance part is given by
Z
-penguins and box diagrams.
However, this decay
is dominated by a long-distance contribution from
a two-photon intermediate state.
In fact, the full branching ratio can be written as
Buchalla:1995vs
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})=|\text{Re}A|^{2}+|\text{Im}A|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\text{Re}A=A_{\text{SD}}+A_{\text{LD}}

(270)

with Re
A
and Im
A
denoting the dispersive and absorptive contributions, respectively.
The absorptive (imaginary) part of the long-distance component is determined by the measured rate for
K_{L}\rightarrow\gamma\gamma
to be

\text{Br}_{\text{abs}}(K_{L}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})=(6.64\pm 0.07)\cdot 10^{-9}
and it almost
completely saturates the observed rate
Br
(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}=(6.84\pm 0.11)\cdot 10^{-9}

PDG18
.
The real part of the long-distance amplitude cannot be derived directly from experiment.
However in ref.
Isidori
it is estimated an upper bound
on the short distance contribution:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SD}}<2.5\cdot 10^{-9}

(271)

As shown below,
the SM prediction for the short distance contribution results:

\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SD,SM}}\approx 0.9\cdot 10^{-9}

Buras:1997fb
. Then the condition (
271
) provides a constraint
on new physics scenarios.

Since only the axial component of both hadronic and lepton current contributes to the decay,
the effective Lagrangian describing the short distance contribution to the decay in the SM
can be written as
Buchalla:1995vs
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM,SD}}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\alpha(M_{Z})}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{\text{W}}}\big(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd}Y(x_{c})+V_{ts}^{*}V_{td}Y(x_{t})\big)(\overline{s}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}d)(\overline{\mu}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\mu)+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{F}_{L2}(\overline{s}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}d)(\overline{\mu}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\mu)+\text{h.c.}

(272)

Y(x_{a})
,
x_{a}=m^{2}_{a}/M^{2}_{W}
,
are the Inami-Lim functions including QCD and electroweak corrections,
whose leading order term is a linear combination of the axial components of
Z
-penguins and box-diagrams.
Numerically
Y(x_{c})\approx 3.3\times 10^{-4}
,
Y(x_{t})\approx 0.97

Buchalla:1995vs
.
In eq. (
272
) we defined the constant

\mathcal{F}_{L2}\approx(-2.1+i\,0.78)\times 10^{-6}
.

In the scenario with extra isosinglet quark,
the non-diagonal couplings of
Z
-boson with SM families due to the mixing of
light quarks with the
b^{\prime}
-quark
leads to the Lagrangian analogous to (
266
), contributing to the decay

K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
at tree level (figure
7
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}(\overline{s}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}d)\frac{1}{2}(\overline{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}\mu)+\text{h.c.}

(273)

By using again the phase convention
C|K^{0}\rangle=+|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle

we have:

\displaystyle\langle 0|\bar{d}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}s|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle=+\langle 0|\bar{s}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}d|K^{0}\rangle

(275)

and neglecting indirect CP violation:

\displaystyle K_{L}\approx K_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(K^{0}+\bar{K}^{0})

(276)

Then we get:

\displaystyle\langle\mu^{+}\mu^{-}|\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM,SD}}|K_{L}\rangle=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{2\text{Re}(\mathcal{F}_{L2})}{\sqrt{2}}\langle 0|\bar{s}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}d|K^{0}\rangle[\bar{u}(p^{(\mu)})\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}v(p^{(\bar{\mu})})]

(277)

\displaystyle\langle\mu^{+}\mu^{-}|\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}|K_{L}\rangle=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\text{Re}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})}{\sqrt{2}}\langle 0|\bar{s}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}d|K^{0}\rangle[\bar{u}(p^{(\mu)})\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}v(p^{(\bar{\mu})})]

(278)

Then we can define the branching ratio given by the amplitude of the short distance contribution:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SD}}\!=k\left[\text{Re}(2\mathcal{F}_{L2}+V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})\right]^{2},

\displaystyle k\!=\!\frac{\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\nu_{\mu})\tau_{K_{L}}M_{K^{0}}\sqrt{1\!-\!4\frac{m^{2}_{\mu}}{M^{2}_{K^{0}}}}}{\tau(K^{+})M_{K^{+}}\left(1\!-\!\frac{m^{2}_{\mu}}{M^{2}_{K^{+}}}\right)^{2}|V_{us}|^{2}}

(279)

where we have used
\langle 0|\bar{s}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}d|K^{0}\rangle=ip_{\mu}f_{K}
,

\text{Br}(K^{+}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\nu_{\mu})=0.6356(11)
and
|V_{us}|=0.2252
. In absence of new physics, the short distance contribution coincides with the SM expectation

\text{Br}(K_{\text{L}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SD,SM}}
.

By using the upper bound in eq. (
271
) on the branching ratio (
279
)
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\text{Re}(2\mathcal{F}_{L2}+V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})\right|

\displaystyle<6.9\times 10^{-6}

(280)

This constraint is shown in figure
13
in terms of the modulus
|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|

and relative phase
\delta_{L21}^{d}
, as parameterized in (
4
), (
211
).
In terms of the ratio between the new amplitude (
273
) and
the SM amplitude (
272
), eq. (
280
) implies:

\displaystyle\left|1+\frac{\text{Re}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})}{2\text{Re}(\mathcal{F}_{L2})}\right|<1.7\,,\qquad 0.7<\frac{|\text{Re}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})|}{2|\text{Re}\mathcal{F}_{L2}|}<2.7

(281)

or, by using
\text{Re}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})=|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|\cos\delta^{d}_{L21}
we have:

\displaystyle-0.28\times 10^{-5}<|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|\cos\delta^{d}_{L21}<1.11\times 10^{-5}\,.

(282)

where we used the Wolfenstein parameterization (
245
).

4.1.5
K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}

The effective Lagrangian (
272
) gives the short distance contribution to
the decay
K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SD,SM}}=4k_{K_{S}}\left[\text{Im}(\mathcal{F}_{L2})\right]^{2}

(283)

where
k_{K_{S}}
is the same as
k
in eq. (
279
) with the change

\tau_{K_{L}}\rightarrow\tau_{K_{S}}
.
It is obtained
\text{Br}(K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SD}}\approx 0.2\cdot 10^{-12}
.
There are also long distance contributions arising from the two photon intermediate state
which result in the rate

\text{Br}(K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{LD}}\approx 5.1\cdot 10^{-12}

Cirigliano
.
The experimental upper limit for this decay was found by
the LHCb collaboration

LHCbmumupub
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}<1.0\cdot 10^{-9}\quad(95\%\,\text{C.L.})

(284)

Then,
an extra contribution
is allowed to be higher than both
the short and long-distance contributions to the decay, so we can impose an upper limit on the
new contribution arising in the scenario with the extra isosinglet.
The new decay channel is described by the effective Lagrangian
(
273
) and gives the rate:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{new}}=k_{K_{S}}\left[\text{Im}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})\right]^{2}<\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}

(285)

We have:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(K_{\text{S}}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{new}}<1.0\cdot 10^{-9}

(286)

\displaystyle|\text{Im}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})|=|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}||\sin\delta^{d}_{L21}|<1.0\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\text{Br}_{\text{exp}}}{1.0\cdot 10^{-9}}\right]^{1/2}

(287)

where we used the parameterization in eqs. (
4
), (
211
).

4.2
Limits from neutral mesons systems

4.2.1
K^{0}
-
\bar{K}^{0}
mixing

Figure 8:
Box diagram for the transition
K^{0}\rightarrow\bar{K}^{0}
in the SM,

q_{\alpha},q_{\beta}=u,c,t
.

In the SM the short-distance contribution to the transition

K^{0}(d\bar{s})\leftrightarrow\bar{K}^{0}(\bar{d}s)

arises from weak box diagrams (figure
8
).
The effective Lagrangian describing this contribution is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{SM}}_{\Delta S=2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{4\pi^{2}}\left[\lambda^{2}_{c}S_{0}(x_{c})+\lambda^{2}_{t}S_{0}(x_{t})+2\lambda_{c}\lambda_{t}S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t})\right](\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(288)

where
\lambda_{a}=V_{as}^{*}V_{ad}
,
x_{a}=\frac{m_{a}^{2}}{m_{W}^{2}}
and
S_{0}(x_{i})
are the Inami-Lim
functions
Inami:1980fz
:

\displaystyle S_{0}(x)=x

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4-11x+x^{2}}{4(1-x)^{2}}-\frac{3x^{2}\ln x}{2(1-x)^{3}}\right)

(289)

\displaystyle S_{0}(x_{j},x_{k})=x_{j}x_{k}

\displaystyle\left[\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2(x_{j}-1)}-\frac{3}{4(x_{j}-1)^{2}}\right)\frac{\log x_{j}}{x_{j}-x_{k}}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2(x_{k}-1)}-\frac{3}{4(x_{k}-1)^{2}}\right)\frac{\log x_{k}}{x_{k}-x_{j}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{3}{4(x_{j}-1)(x_{k}-1)}\right]

(290)

which, being
x_{c}=m_{c}^{2}/m_{W}^{2}\ll 1
, for the c-quark contribution reduce to:

\displaystyle S_{0}(x_{c})\approx x_{c}

(291)

\displaystyle S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t})\approx x_{c}\left[\ln\frac{x_{t}}{x_{c}}-\frac{3x_{t}}{4(1-x_{t})}-\frac{3x_{t}^{2}\ln x_{t}}{4(1-x_{t})^{2}}\right]

(292)

keeping only linear terms in
x_{c}

Buchalla:1995vs
.

The weak short-distance contribution to the mass splitting
\Delta m_{K}=m_{K_{L}}-m_{K_{S}}

and the CP-violating parameter
\epsilon_{K}
are described by
the off-diagonal term
M_{12}
of the mass matrix of neutral kaons, which is given by:

\displaystyle M_{12}=-\frac{1}{2m_{K^{0}}}\langle K^{0}|\mathcal{L}_{\Delta S=2}|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle

(293)

The hadronic matrix element in vacuum insertion approximation (VIA) is written as:

\displaystyle\langle K^{0}|(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle=\frac{2}{3}f^{2}_{K}m^{2}_{K^{0}}

(294)

with the phase choice
CP|K^{0}\rangle=-|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle

Branco
.
f_{K}
is the kaon decay constant, which can be estimated in lattice QCD to be

f_{K}=155.7(0.7)
MeV
FLAG2019
, and
m_{K^{0}}
is the neutral kaons mass. Then, the SM Lagrangian gives:

\displaystyle M_{12}^{\text{SM}}

\displaystyle=\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{12\pi^{2}}(\eta_{1}\lambda^{*2}_{c}S_{0}(x_{c})+\eta_{2}\lambda^{*2}_{t}S_{0}(x_{t})+2\eta_{3}\lambda^{*}_{c}\lambda^{*}_{t}S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t}))f^{2}_{K}m_{K^{0}}B_{K}

(295)

where we also added the factors

\eta_{1}=1.38\pm 0.20
,
\eta_{2}=0.57\pm 0.01
,
\eta_{3}=0.47\pm 0.04
which
describe short-distance QCD effects
Buchalla:1995vs
.
The factor
B_{K}
is the correction to the VIA which is estimated from
lattice QCD calculations, giving

B_{K}=0.7625(97)

FLAG2019
.

The modulus
|M_{12}|
and the imaginary part
\text{Im}M_{12}

respectively describe short-distance contributions in
the mass splitting and CP-violation in
\bar{K}^{0}\rightarrow K^{0}
transition.
In the SM,
in the standard parameterization of
V_{\text{CKM}}

we have:

\displaystyle|M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|\,\approx\,|\text{Re}M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|\,\approx\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{12\pi^{2}}\eta_{1}

\displaystyle|\lambda^{2}_{c}|S_{0}(x_{c})\approx\frac{G^{2}_{F}}{12\pi^{2}}\eta_{1}|\lambda^{2}_{c}|m^{2}_{c}f^{2}_{K}m_{K^{0}}B_{K}

(296)

\displaystyle\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{SM}}=-\frac{G_{F}^{2}m_{W}^{2}}{6\pi^{2}}f^{2}_{K}m_{K^{0}}B_{K}

\displaystyle\left[\eta_{1}\text{Re}(\lambda_{c})\text{Im}(\lambda_{c})S_{0}(x_{c})+\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\big(\eta_{2}\text{Re}(\lambda_{t})S_{0}(x_{t})+\eta_{3}\text{Re}(\lambda_{c})S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t})\big)\text{Im}(\lambda_{t})\right]

(297)

The mass difference between mass eigenstates is given by

Buchalla:1995vs
:

\displaystyle\Delta m_{K}\approx 2|M_{12}|+\Delta m_{\text{LD}}

(298)

where
\Delta m_{\text{LD}}
is the long-distance contribution, which is difficult to evaluate.
Nevertheless, the short distance contribution

\Delta m_{K}\approx 2|M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|\simeq 2.2\cdot 10^{-15}\,\text{GeV}
gives a dominant contribution to
the experimentally measured value
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\Delta M_{K}=(3.484\pm 0.006)\times 10^{-15}\,\text{GeV}

(299)

The CP violation is parameterized by
\epsilon_{K}
, which,
with the phase choice
CP|K^{0}\rangle=-|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle
,
is almost determined by short distance physics

Branco
:

\displaystyle|\epsilon_{K}|\approx\frac{|\text{Im}[M_{12}]|}{\sqrt{2}\Delta m_{K}}

(300)

in the standard parameterization of
V_{\text{CKM}}
.
From experimental data it is obtained

PDG18
:

\displaystyle|\epsilon_{K}|=(2.228\pm 0.011)\times 10^{-3}

(301)

Figure 9:
Contributions to
\bar{K}^{0}
-
K^{0}
transition due to the insertion of
the additional vectorlike down-type weak isosinglet couple
b^{\prime}_{L},b^{\prime}_{R}
in quark mixing.

In our BSM scenario with extra vectorlike
b^{\prime}
quark,
the non-diagonal couplings of
Z
and Higgs bosons with light quarks
in eqs. (
4
), (
4
)
induce the same operator as in (
288
), both at tree level and loop level,
interfering with the SM in
the transition
\bar{K}^{0}\leftrightarrow K^{0}
, as shown in figure
9
.

The new tree level contribution translates into the effective Lagrangian:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{tree}}_{\Delta S=2}=

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(302)

However if
M_{b^{\prime}}
is of order few TeV, the loop contribution to
\bar{K}^{0}
-
K^{0}
mixing
becomes important. In particular, for
M_{b^{\prime}}=3.1
TeV the
effective operator for the
box diagram with Higgs and
Z
bosons exchange in figure
9

gives the same contribution as the effective operator describing the tree level diagram.
Moreover the box diagram contribution
grows as
\propto M^{2}_{b^{\prime}}
.
In fact, the effective Lagrangian describing the loop contribution in figure
9
is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{box}}_{\Delta S=2}\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{(h_{d1}h_{d2}^{*})^{2}}{128\pi^{2}M^{2}_{b^{\prime}}}(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(303)

where
h_{di}
are the Yukawa couplings defined in eq. (
122
).
However, we are interested in fixing a value for the elements of the mixing matrix rather than
setting the Yukawas
h_{di}
. Then, it is convenient to express the Lagrangian explicitly
in terms of the mixing elements.
From eqs. (
211
), (
213
), we have:

\displaystyle h_{d1}\approx-V_{L4d}^{*}\frac{M_{b^{\prime}}}{v_{w}}\,,\qquad h_{d2}\approx-V_{L4s}^{*}\frac{M_{b^{\prime}}}{v_{w}}\,.

(304)

Then, using
G_{F}/\sqrt{2}=1/4v_{w}^{2}
,
the Lagrangian can be written as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{box}}_{\Delta S=2}\approx-\frac{G^{2}_{F}\,M^{2}_{b^{\prime}}}{16\pi^{2}}(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(305)

Hence, the complete new contribution is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta S=2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\text{tree}}_{\Delta S=2}+\mathcal{L}^{\text{box}}_{\Delta S=2}\approx

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}\left(1+\frac{G_{F}M^{2}_{b^{\prime}}}{8\sqrt{2}\pi^{2}}\right)(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}f(M_{b^{\prime}})(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(306)

\displaystyle f(M_{b^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=1+\left(\frac{M_{b^{\prime}}}{3.1\,\text{TeV}}\right)^{2}

(307)

where we defined the function
f(M_{b^{\prime}})
of the mass of the extra quark
b^{\prime}
.
From the Lagrangian (
4.2.1
),
there is an additive contribution
M_{12}^{\text{new}}
to the mixing mass
M_{12}
(see eq. (
293
)), participating
both in the mass splitting and CP-violating effects. Then, the new contribution is constrained by both CP-conserving and CP-violating observables.

Regarding the CP-conserving part, which originates the mass splitting
\Delta m_{K}
,
we can confront the moduli of the two components of the mixing mass and
impose that the new contribution
|M_{12}^{\text{new}}|

is less than a fraction
\Delta_{K}
of the short-distance SM contribution

|M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|
:

\displaystyle|M_{12}^{\text{new}}|<|M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|\,\Delta_{K}

(308)

where we defined real and positive
\Delta_{K}
.
This is analogous to comparing the modulus of the coefficients of the effective operators in
eqs. (
288
) and (
4.2.1
). We evaluate the constraint at leading order of both SM and new physics contributions,
neglecting QCD corrections.
Hence, eq. (
308
) corresponds to:

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}f(M_{b^{\prime}})\left|V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s}\right|^{2}<\,\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{c}}{4\pi^{2}}|\lambda^{2}_{c}|\,\Delta_{K}

(309)

eq. (
308
) can also be translated into a constraint on the scale of the new contribution:

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}f(M_{b^{\prime}})\left|(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}\right|<\frac{1}{\>\Lambda_{sd}^{2}}\,,\qquad\Delta_{K}=\left(\frac{1.9\cdot 10^{3}\,\text{TeV}}{\Lambda_{ds}}\right)^{2}

(310)

where we defined the minimum allowed equivalent scale
\Lambda_{sd}^{2}
of the new contribution.
We estimate the constraint with
\Delta_{K}=1
, that is by imposing the condition

|M_{12}^{\text{new}}|<|M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|
, meaning that
the additional processes contribute to
\Delta m_{K}
at most as the SM
short-distance contribution.
Anyway, we leave
\Delta_{K}
as free parameter in next equations
so to allow to reevaluate the results with a different constraint.
Then, using
eqs. (
4
), (
211
),
from the condition (
309
) we have:

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s}|<1.7\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{f(1\,\text{TeV})}{f(M_{b^{\prime}})}\right]^{1/2}\left[\Delta_{K}\right]^{1/2}

(311)

where we evaluated the constraint for the benchmark value
M_{b^{\prime}}=1
TeV.

Regarding the contribution to the CP-violating parameter
\epsilon_{K}
,
the imaginary part of the new contribution to the mixing mass
\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{new}}

can be constrained to be a fraction
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}
of the SM contribution:

\displaystyle|\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{new}}|<|\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|\,\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}\,,

(312)

where we defined real and positive
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}
.
At leading order of the new physics contribution, eq. (
312
) is equivalent to comparing the magnitude of the imaginary part of operators
(
288
) and (
4.2.1
):

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}f(M_{b^{\prime}})|\text{Im}[(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}]|<

\displaystyle\,\frac{G_{F}^{2}m^{2}_{W}}{4\pi^{2}}2\left|\eta_{1}\text{Re}(\lambda_{c})\text{Im}(\lambda_{c})S_{0}(x_{c})+\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\big(\eta_{2}\text{Re}(\lambda_{t})S_{0}(x_{t})+\eta_{3}\text{Re}(\lambda_{c})S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t})\big)\text{Im}(\lambda_{t})\right|\,\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}

(313)

eq. (
313
) can also be translated into a constraint on the scale of the new contribution:

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}f(M_{b^{\prime}})\left|\text{Im}[(V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s})^{2}]\right|<\frac{1}{\>\Lambda_{sd,\text{Im}}^{2}}\,,\qquad\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}=\left(\frac{1.6\cdot 10^{4}\,\text{TeV}}{\Lambda_{ds,\text{Im}}}\right)^{2}

(314)

where we defined the minimum allowed equivalent scale
\Lambda_{sd,\text{Im}}^{2}
of the new contribution.
The numerical value is obtained by using the Wolfenstein parameterization as in eq. (
245
), with
\text{Im}(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})=-A^{2}\lambda^{5}\eta
.
We make an estimation choosing
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}=0.4
,
corresponding to
\Lambda_{ds,\text{Im}}=2.5\cdot 10^{4}\,\text{TeV}
.
Using eqs. (
4
) and (
211
), from the condition (
313
) we have:

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4s}|\sqrt{|\sin[2(\delta^{d}_{L12})]|}<1.3\cdot 10^{-5}\left[\frac{f(1\,\text{TeV})}{f(M_{b^{\prime}})}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}}{0.4}\right]^{1/2}

(315)

where we evaluated the constraint for the benchmark value
M_{b^{\prime}}=1
TeV.
The constraints are also
shown in figure

for

.

Let us elaborate a little more about the two contributions to
\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta S=2}

from the tree and box diagrams.
The tree level operator (
4.2.1
) can be written in terms of the Yukawa constants
h_{di}
,
in order to be compared with
the operator (

).
Using
G_{F}/\sqrt{2}=1/4v_{w}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{tree}}_{\Delta S=2}=-\frac{(h_{d1}h_{d2}^{*})^{2}}{4}\frac{v_{w}^{2}}{M_{b^{\prime}}^{4}}(\overline{d_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(316)

which parametrically is a factor
v_{w}^{2}/M_{b^{\prime}}^{2}
less than the box operator (
303
).
That is why, after compensating

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}_{d,s}^{0}|(\overline{b_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}q_{L})^{2}|B^{0}_{d,s}\rangle=\frac{2}{3}f^{2}_{B_{d,s}}M^{2}_{B^{0}_{d,s}}

\displaystyle 2M_{B^{0}_{d,s}}M_{12}^{(d,s)*}=\langle\bar{B}_{d,s}^{0}|-\mathcal{L}_{\Delta B(d,s)=2}|B^{0}_{d,s}\rangle

\displaystyle\Delta M_{d,s}=2|M_{12}^{(d,s)}|

Putting together (

), (

\displaystyle\Delta M_{d,s}^{\text{SM}}=\frac{G_{F}^{2}m_{W}^{2}}{6\pi^{2}}f^{2}_{B_{d,s}}m_{B_{d,s}}B_{B_{d,s}}|(V_{tb}V_{td/s}^{*})^{2}|\eta_{B}S_{0}(x_{t})

and

\displaystyle\Delta M_{s\,\text{exp}}=(1.1683\pm 0.0013)\cdot 10^{-11}\,\text{GeV}

In our BSM scenario with extra vectorlike

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta B(s)=2}\approx-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4b})^{2}f(M_{b^{\prime}})(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}b_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle f(M_{b^{\prime}})=1+\left(\frac{M_{b^{\prime}}}{3.1\,\text{TeV}}\right)^{2}

(327)

From the Lagrangians (

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}f(M_{b^{\prime}})|V_{L4d}^{*}V_{L4b}|^{2}<\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}m^{2}_{W}|V_{td}|^{2}S(x_{t})\cdot\Delta_{B_{d}}=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{bd}^{2}}

where we evaluate the constraint at leading order of both SM and new physics contributions,

\displaystyle|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4b}|<1.6\times 10^{-3}\left[\frac{f(1\,\text{TeV})}{f(M_{b^{\prime}})}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{\Delta_{B_{s}}}{0.3}\right]^{1/2}

4.2.3
D^{0}
-
\bar{D}^{0}
mixing

In the SM
D^{0}(c\bar{u})
-
\bar{D}^{0}(\bar{c}u)
mixing receives contributions from box diagrams,
dipenguin diagrams and from long-distance effects
Branco
.
In box diagrams only the internal strange and down quarks contribute effectively.
Differently from the case of kaons and B-mesons, the masses of
s
and
d
are small compared to the mass of the external
c
quarks, which then cannot be neglected and
an extra operator appears in the effective Lagrangian. In VIA approximation,
the box contribution results
|M_{12}|\sim 10^{-17}\div 10^{-16}
GeV
Branco
.
Also dipenguin diagrams can give a contribution not much smaller than box diagrams
Petrov
.
Long-distance effects are expected to be large
Branco
.
However, since their contribution is non-perturbative and cannot be computed reliably,
there is room for new physics, which in principle can be the dominant contribution to
the mass difference
\Delta m_{D}
in
D^{0}
system.
In fact, the experimental value of the mass difference in the
D^{0}
mesons system is
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}=(6.25^{+2.70}_{-2.90})\times 10^{-15}\text{GeV}

(332)

which at
95\%
C.L.
allows values two orders of magnitude bigger
than the SM short-distance expectation.

Figure 12:
New contribution to
D^{0}-\bar{D^{0}}
mixing,
q=d,s,b,b^{\prime}
.

In the scenario with extra down-type quark,
the additional box diagrams with internal
b^{\prime}
quarks contribute to
D
mesons mixing, as shown in
figure (
12
). The corresponding effective Lagrangian is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta C=2}\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{4\pi^{2}}(V_{ub^{\prime}}V_{cb^{\prime}}^{*})^{2}S_{0}(x_{b^{\prime}})(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}c_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(333)

with
x_{b^{\prime}}=\frac{M_{b^{\prime}}^{2}}{m_{W}^{2}}
(taking
M_{b^{\prime}}\simeq 1
TeV, the two mixed contributions
would be competitive only if
|V_{ub^{\prime}}V_{cb^{\prime}}^{*}|<2\cdot 10^{-7}
, but we are considering scenarios with larger mixings).
Since the new effective operator (
333
) originates from
charged currents, this process involves directly
the elements
V_{ub^{\prime}}
,
V_{cb^{\prime}}
of the enlarged CKM matrix
\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}
(
4
).
This operator gives an additional contribution
M^{D}_{12\,\text{new}}

to the mixing mass
and thus contributes to the mass difference
\Delta m_{D}
:

\displaystyle\Delta m_{D\text{new}}=2|M^{D}_{12\,\text{new}}|\simeq\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{6\pi^{2}}(V_{ub^{\prime}}V_{cb^{\prime}}^{*})^{2}S_{0}(x_{b^{\prime}})f^{2}_{D}m_{D^{0}}

(334)

where
f_{D}=212.0\pm 0.7
MeV
FLAG2019
is the decay constant.
Since the new contribution can be regarded as
the dominant one, we can think that the new operator can account up to the entire
mass difference in D-mesons system. Then we can set a constraint directly on the new contribution.
We can constrain
\Delta m_{D\,\text{new}}
by using
use the
95\%
C.L. experimental limit obtained from eq. (
332
):

\displaystyle\Delta m_{D\text{new}}<\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}=1.2\times 10^{-14}\,\text{GeV}

(335)

\displaystyle|V_{ub^{\prime}}V_{cb^{\prime}}^{*}|<4.6\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}}{1.2\cdot 10^{-14}}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}\right]

(336)

The last scaling in eq. (
336
) holds since
S_{0}(x_{b^{\prime}})\sim\frac{1}{4}x_{b^{\prime}}
for
M_{b^{\prime}}\gtrsim 2
TeV.

4.3
Limits from rare
B
mesons decays

4.3.1
Rare semileptonic B decays

Results on rare B-decays can constrain mixings of the extra vector-like quark with SM families
because of the new contributions to
FCNC processes involving
b\rightarrow s(d)

originated at tree level by non-diagonal couplings of
Z
-boson
with SM quarks.

The new effective Lagrangian contributing to
b\rightarrow s(d)\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
decays are:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}(\overline{b_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})\left[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})(\overline{\ell_{L}}\gamma_{\mu}\ell_{L})+\sin^{2}\theta_{W}(\overline{\ell_{R}}\gamma_{\mu}\ell_{R})\right]

(337)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}(\overline{b_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})\left[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})(\overline{\ell_{L}}\gamma_{\mu}\ell_{L})+\sin^{2}\theta_{W}(\overline{\ell_{R}}\gamma_{\mu}\ell_{R})\right]

(338)

giving both exclusive decays, such as
B\rightarrow\pi\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
and
B\rightarrow K\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
, and
inclusive decays
B\rightarrow X_{s(d)}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
,
where
X_{s(d)}
stands for anything with a
s(d)
quark.

As regards exclusive decays,
experimental branching fractions are below SM predictions
PDG18
.
The decays
B^{0}\rightarrow K^{0*}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
and
B^{+}\rightarrow K^{+}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}

are the best studied and tensions with SM were found related to the quantity
P^{\prime}_{5}
and to lepton universality test

PDG18
(and references therein).

Inclusive decays are also studied in order to avoid hadronic form factors.
As regards
B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
,
branching fractions can be analyzed both from a theoretical and experimental point of view in
low and high dilepton invariant mass regions, that is

1\,\text{GeV}^{2}<q^{2}<6\,\text{GeV}^{2}
and

q^{2}>14.4\text{GeV}^{2}
regions (the intermediate region is dominated by charmonia resonances).
Experimental measurements of the inclusive decay were made by both
Belle
Belle
and BaBar
BaBar
. Their
data are consistent with SM expectations at
95\%
C.L.
Huber:2015sra
.
In order to have a rough estimate on the constraint for the mixings

|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|
,
|V_{L4s}V_{L4b}^{*}|
, we can consider the
total branching fraction averaged between electrons and muons, which experimentally
results
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{exp}}=(5.8\pm 1.3)\times 10^{-6}

(339)

SM calculations predict
\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{SM}}=(4.6\pm 0.8)\times 10^{-6}

Ghinculov:2003qd
.
Let us define
the branching ratio which would be given only by the new contribution:

\displaystyle|A_{\text{new}}|^{2}=\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{new}}\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{c}\ell^{+}\nu_{\ell})\frac{|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}|^{2}[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})^{2}+\sin^{4}\theta_{W}]}{|V_{cb}|^{2}}<\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{max}}

(340)

\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{max}}
is defined here as the maximal allowed value
for the branching ratio generated by the amplitude of the new contribution alone and we use the experimental branching ratio

\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{c}\ell^{+}\nu_{\ell})=0.1065(16)

PDG18
.
Similarly
|A_{\text{SM}}|^{2}=\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{SM}}
. Then, at
95\%
C.L.
of the experimental result (
339
), we should have:

\displaystyle 3.25\times 10^{-6}<|A_{\text{new}}+A_{\text{SM}}|^{2}<8.35\times 10^{-6}

(341)

Then, in order to set the least stringent constraint, we can consider the case of destructive interference:

\displaystyle|A_{\text{new}}|<\sqrt{8.35\times 10^{-6}}\left(1+\frac{|A_{\text{SM}}|}{\sqrt{8.35\times 10^{-6}}}\right)

(342)

By using the
95\%
C.L. interval also for the SM branching ratio,
|A_{\text{SM}}|^{2}=6.2\times 10^{-6}
,
the resulting constraint is:

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}|<1.9\times 10^{-3}\left[\frac{\text{Br}(B\rightarrow X_{s}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{max}}}{2.9\times 10^{-5}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(343)

and, for this case of destructive interference,
|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}|>1.5\cdot 10^{-3}
.

As regards
b\rightarrow d\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
transitions,
the LHCb collaboration measured the branching ratio for the decay

B^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\mu^{+}\mu^{-}

PDG18
,
LHCbpi0
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}=(1.75\pm 0.22)\cdot 10^{-8}

(344)

which agrees with SM predictions, for example

\text{Br}(B^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SM}}=1.88^{+0.32}_{-0.21}\cdot 10^{-8}

Ali:2013zfa

\text{Br}(B^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SM}}=(2.04\pm 0.21)\cdot 10^{-8}

Bailey:2015nbd
.
Other than that, upper limits on exclusive decays include
Belle constraint
\text{Br}(B^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})<4.9\cdot 10^{-8}
(
90\%
C.L.)

Belle3
and BaBar result
BaBar3
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{exp}}<5.3\cdot 10^{-8}\qquad 90\%\,\text{C.L.}

(345)

\ell=e
or
\mu
.
The SM expectation for the
B^{0}
decay can be obtained from the predictions of
B^{+}

branching ratios by multiplying by the factor
(\tau_{B^{0}}/\tau_{B^{+}})/2
, where

\tau_{B^{0}}/\tau_{B^{+}}=1.076(4)

PDG18
.
Let us define as before the decay rate which would arise only from the new contribution:

\displaystyle|A^{\text{new}}_{B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}|^{2}=\text{Br}(B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{new}}\simeq k_{B^{\pm}}|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|^{2}

(346)

\displaystyle|A^{\text{new}}_{B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}|^{2}=\text{Br}(B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{new}}\simeq k_{B^{0}}|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|^{2}

(347)

with

\displaystyle k_{B^{\pm}}=2\frac{\text{Br}(B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\nu_{\ell})[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})^{2}+\sin^{4}\theta_{W}]}{|V_{ub}|^{2}}

(348)

\displaystyle k_{B^{0}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{Br}(B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{-}\ell^{+}\nu_{\ell})[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})^{2}+\sin^{4}\theta_{W}]}{|V_{ub}|^{2}}

(349)

The factors of
2
take into account the isospin symmetry relation.
We also take advantage of the
results in
PDG18

\text{Br}(B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{-}\ell^{+}\nu_{\ell})=(1.50\pm 0.06)\cdot 10^{-4}
,

\text{Br}(B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\nu_{\ell})=(7.80\pm 0.27)\cdot 10^{-5}
,
\ell=e
or
\mu
.
Then we should have:

\displaystyle 1.3\times 10^{-8}<\,

\displaystyle|A_{B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{new}}+A_{B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|^{2}<2.2\times 10^{-8}

(350)

\displaystyle|A_{B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{new}}+A_{B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|^{2}<5.3\cdot 10^{-8}

(351)

where we defined
|A_{\text{SM}}|^{2}=\text{Br}_{\text{SM}}
. We used the
95\%
C.L. of the
experimental result (
344
) and the upper limit (
345
).
In order to set the constraint, we can consider the case of destructive interference between the new
contribution and the SM one:

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|<\frac{|A_{B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|}{\sqrt{k_{B^{\pm}}}}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{2.2\times 10^{-8}}}{|A_{B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|}\right)

(352)

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|<\sqrt{\frac{5.3\times 10^{-8}}{k_{B^{0}}}}\left(1+\frac{|A_{B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|}{\sqrt{5.3\times 10^{-8}}}\right)

(353)

In order to have the least stringent constraint, we can use the
95\%
C.L. interval of the SM expectations,

|A_{B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|^{2}=2.5\times 10^{-8}
and

|A_{B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}}^{\text{SM}}|^{2}=1.3\times 10^{-8}
. Then, we obtain:

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|<2.6\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\text{Br}(B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{max}}}{9.3\cdot 10^{-8}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(354)

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}V_{L4b}^{*}|<4.3\cdot 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\text{Br}(B^{0}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\ell^{+}\ell^{-})_{\text{max}}}{1.2\cdot 10^{-7}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(355)

where
\text{Br}_{\text{max}}
is defined as in eq. (
4.3.1
) as the maximal allowed value
for the branching ratio generated by the amplitude of the new contribution alone.
Regarding the measured decay
B^{\pm}\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
,
in this case of destructive interference, we should also have
|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4d}|>2.3\cdot 10^{-4}
.

4.3.2
B^{0}_{d,s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}

The decays
B^{0}_{d,s}\rightarrow\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
,
\ell=e,\mu,\tau
,
are dominated by the
Z
-penguin and box diagrams involving top quark exchanges.
The charm contributions are fully negligible here and the
effective Lagrangian in the SM is
Buras:1997fb
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{SM}}=\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{2\pi^{2}}V_{tb}^{*}V_{tq}Y(x_{t})(\overline{b}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}q)(\bar{\ell}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\ell)

(356)

with
q=d,s
.

Y(x_{t})
is the Inami-Lim function, including QCD and electroweak corrections,
which at leading order is a linear combination of Z-penguin and box diagrams,
the same as in eq. (
272
). Here we use
Y(x_{t})=0.935
from ref.
Bobeth:2013uxa
, inserting the values in table
4
.

The non-diagonal couplings of
Z
-boson with SM families lead to the same extra tree level contributions as in eqs. (
337
), (
338
). In this case only the axial part of both quark and leptons is involved:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{2}V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4q}(\overline{b}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}q)(\bar{\ell}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\ell)

(357)

for
q=d,s
.
Considering the case
\ell=\mu
, the branching ratios of the decays

B^{0}_{d,s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
would be:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}_{d}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{tot}}=k_{d}\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2}\left|\frac{\alpha}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{W}}Y(x_{t})V_{tb}^{*}V_{td}+\frac{1}{2}V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4d}\right|^{2}

(358)

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}_{s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{tot}}=k_{s}\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2}\left|\frac{\alpha}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{W}}Y(x_{t})V_{tb}^{*}V_{ts}+\frac{1}{2}V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}\right|^{2}

(359)

where
B_{q}
is the flavour eigenstate
(\bar{b}q)
,

f_{B_{s}}=230.3(1.3)
MeV,
f_{B_{d}}=190.0(1.3)
MeV
FLAG2019
are the decay
constants defined by

\langle 0|\bar{b}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}q|B^{0}_{q}(p)\rangle=ip_{\mu}f_{B_{q}}
, and:

\displaystyle k_{d}=\tau_{B^{0}_{d}}\frac{1}{2\pi}f^{2}_{B_{d}}m^{2}_{\mu}M_{B^{0}_{d}}\sqrt{1-4m_{\mu}^{2}/M^{2}_{B^{0}_{d}}}\>,

\displaystyle k_{s}=\tau_{B^{0}_{sH}}\frac{1}{2\pi}f^{2}_{B_{s}}m^{2}_{\mu}M_{B^{0}_{s}}\sqrt{1-4m_{\mu}^{2}/M^{2}_{B^{0}_{s}}}

(360)

In absence of new physics, the branching ratios (
359
), (
358
)
give the SM expectations
which are respectively:

\text{Br}(B^{0}_{d}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SM}}\approx 8.6\times 10^{-11}
,
\text{Br}(B^{0}_{s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{SM}}\approx 3.2\times 10^{-9}
,
obtained using the central values of the quantities in table
4
.

The experimental branching ratio of the decay
B^{0}_{s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
is
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}_{s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}=(3.0\pm 0.4)\times 10^{-9}

(361)

which is in agreement with the SM expectation.
Regerding the decay
B^{0}_{d}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
, the experimental limit is
Atlas
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}_{d}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}<2.1\times 10^{-10}\qquad 95\%\,\text{C.L.}

(362)

Then, we should set a limit on the decay rates (
358
), (
),
in order to not contradict experimental results. At
95\%
C.L. we can take:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}_{d}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{tot}}<2.1\times 10^{-10}

(363)

\displaystyle 2.2\times 10^{-9}<\;

\displaystyle\text{Br}(B^{0}_{s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{tot}}<3.8\times 10^{-9}

(364)

Depending on the relative unknown phase of the mixing elements
V_{L4b}
,
V_{L4d/s}
,
the above upper limits correspond to:

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4d}|<(0.4\div 2.2)\times 10^{-4}

(365)

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}|<(0.3\div 8.1)\times 10^{-4}

(366)

For the decay
B^{0}_{s}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
, depending on the relative phase,
there is also a lower limit for
-1<\cos(\delta^{d}_{L32})<-0.56
, where

\delta^{d}_{L32}
is the relative phase of the mixing elements
V_{L4b}
,
V_{L4s}

in terms of the parameterization (
4
), (
211
).
in case of negative interference
We can express the limits (
363
), (
364
) in terms of comparison between
the new amplitude and the SM amplitude.
By defining:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{B^{0}_{q}}=\frac{\alpha}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{W}}Y(x_{t})V_{tb}^{*}V_{tq}

(367)

we can constrain the ratio between the magnitude of the two effective operators
(
356
) and (
357
):

\displaystyle\frac{|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4q}|}{2|\mathcal{F}_{B^{0}_{q}}|}<\Delta_{B^{0}_{q}}

(368)

In the case of destructive interference for both the decays,
the limits (
365
), (
366
) correspond to:

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4d}|<2.2\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\Delta_{B^{0}_{d}}}{2.7}\right]\left[\frac{\mathcal{F}_{B^{0}_{d}}}{4.1\times 10^{-5}}\right]

(369)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\Delta_{B^{0}_{s}}}{1.8}\right]7.1\times 10^{-4}<\>

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4s}|<8.1\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\Delta_{B^{0}_{s}}}{2.1}\right]\left[\frac{\mathcal{F}_{B^{0}_{s}}}{2.0\times 10^{-4}}\right]

(370)

for
\delta^{d}_{L31}=2.72
,
\delta^{d}_{L32}=\pi
respectively.

PDG18
.

The presence of additional vector-like quarks also affects
the diagonal couplings of Z-boson with standard quarks,
changing the prediction of many observables related to the
Z
-boson physics e.g.
the
Z
total width
\Gamma_{Z}
,
the partial decay width into hadrons
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})
,
the partial decay widths
R_{c}=\Gamma(c\bar{c})/\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})
,
R_{b}=\Gamma(b\bar{b})/\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})
,
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow q\bar{q})
,
q=u,d,s,c,b
.
Constraints obtained from these quantities are analyzed in the following sections.
Experimental values and SM predictions are taken from
Particle Data Group
PDG18
, as reported in table
5
.

The predicted partial decay width of Z-boson decaying into
b\bar{b}
is

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{SM}}\approx 375.75\mp 0.18
MeV,
which should be compared to the experimental value

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{exp}}\simeq 377.3\mp 1.2
MeV,
where data from PDG
PDG18
are used, as reported in table
5
.
The SM prediction of the partial decay rate of
Z\rightarrow b\bar{b}

at tree level is given by:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{SM}}=\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left[\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}\right]

(371)

In order to compare with the experimental result, we should include QCD corrections, which
are given by a multiplicative factor
\approx 1.021

PDG18
.
By inserting the vector-like isosinglet down-quark, the decay rate at tree level
changes in:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})=\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left[\left(-\frac{1}{2}(1-|V_{L4b}|^{2})+\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}\right]

(372)

which means:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})-\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{SM}}\approx\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)|V_{L4b}|^{2}<0

(373)

So the prediction for the decay rate is lowered, not going towards the direction of
a better agreement with the experimental value. Then, the extra contribution to the rate should be constrained:

\displaystyle\left|\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})-\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{SM}}\right|<\Delta\Gamma_{Zbb}

(374)

We may choose
\Delta\Gamma_{Zbb}

so that
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{tot}}

lays in the
95\%
C.L. interval of the experimental value, which implies:

\displaystyle\left|\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})-\Gamma(Z\rightarrow b\bar{b})_{\text{SM}}\right|<8.6\times 10^{-4}\,\text{GeV}

(375)

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}|<3.2\cdot 10^{-2}\left[\frac{\Delta\Gamma_{Zbb}}{8.6\times 10^{-4}\,\text{GeV}}\right]^{1/2}

(376)

The SM predictions for the Z decay rate and partial decay rate into hadrons are
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z)_{\text{SM}}=2.4942\pm 0.0009\,\text{GeV}\,,\qquad\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{hadr})_{\text{SM}}=1.7411\pm 0.0008\,\text{GeV}

(377)

to be compared with the experimental results
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z)_{\text{exp}}=2.4952\pm 0.0023\,\text{GeV}\,,\qquad\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{hadr})_{\text{exp}}=1.7444\pm 0.0020\,\text{GeV}

(378)

In this BSM scenario,
the deviation from the SM expectation of the new predicted
Z

partial decay rate into hadrons
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})

(which also corresponds to the deviation of the total
Z
decay rate
\Gamma(Z)
,
since there are not additional leptons)
is:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})-\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{SM}}=\Gamma(Z)-\Gamma(Z)_{\text{SM}}=

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left[\sum_{i,j=d,s,b}\left|-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}V_{Lki}^{*}V_{Lkj}+\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\delta_{ij}\right|^{2}-3\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}\right]\approx

\displaystyle\approx\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)\left(|V_{L4d}|^{2}+|V_{L4s}|^{2}+|V_{L4b}|^{2}\right)<0

(379)

QCD corrections should also be included, which amount to a multiplicative factor
\approx 1.041

for
d,s
-quarks and
\approx 1.021
for
b
-quark
PDG18
.
As shown in eq. (
379
),
the prediction for the decay rate is lowered with respect to the SM expectation

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{hadr})_{\text{SM}}
.
Then, since the SM expectation (
377
) is below
the experimental result (
378
), the contribution of the extra quarks is
not leading towards a better agreement. Therefore,
in order to set a constraint on the new expected decay rate,
we can
impose that the new expectation

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})
should be
in the
95\%
C.L. interval of the experimental value
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{exp}}
,
assuming in eq. (
379
) the limit value for the SM prediction

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{SM}}=1.7419
GeV.
That gives:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{SM}}-\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})<\Delta\Gamma_{Z}

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}|^{2}+|V_{L4s}|^{2}+|V_{L4b}|^{2}<1.7\times 10^{-3}\left[\frac{\Delta\Gamma_{Z}}{1.4\times 10^{-3}\,\text{GeV}}\right]

(380)

With
|V_{L4d}|=0.03
, the constraint means
|V_{L4s}|^{2}+|V_{L4b}|^{2}<0.0008
,
which is satisfied for example if both
|V_{L4s}|,|V_{L4b}|<0.02
.
If
V_{L4s}=V_{L4b}=0
, this constraint implies:

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}|^{2}<1.7\times 10^{-3}\;,\qquad\quad|V_{L4d}|<0.041

(381)

which is extremely close to the value needed to solve the CKM unitarity problem
(for example, at
95\%
C.L.
|V_{L4d}|=0.038^{+0.008}_{-0.011}
(
163
)
using our conservative averages for
V_{ud}
and
V_{us}
values (
15
), (
20
)).
This means that an extra weak singlet could not completely explain the CKM unitarity anomaly with more extreme values of the determinations of CKM elements,
as can be seen for example by comparing eq. (
381
)
to the needed values
|V_{L4d}|^{2}=\delta_{\text{CKM}}

displayed in table
2
.

Constraints are expected also from
Z-pole asymmetry analyses of
e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrow ff

processes. In particular,
left-right asymmetries
A_{LR}
, forward-backward asymmetries
A_{FB}

and left-right forward-backward asymmetries
A_{LRFB}

ALEPH:2005ab

were measured at LEP.
Cross sections for
Z
-boson exchange are usually written
in terms of the asymmetry parameters
A_{f}
,

f=e,\mu,\tau,b,c,s,q
, which contain final-state couplings. For example, they are related as

A_{FB}^{(0,f)}=\frac{3}{4}A_{e}A_{f}
,
A_{LRFB}^{(0,f)}=\frac{3}{4}A_{f}
(where the superscript
0
indicates the quantity corrected for radiative effects).
The presence of an additional isosinglet changes the couplings of quarks with the
Z
boson
as in eq. (
4
). Consequently the predictions for the asymmetries are also changed:

\displaystyle A_{q}=\frac{(1-|V_{L4q}|^{2})^{2}-4|Q_{q}|\bar{s}^{2}_{q}(1-|V_{L4q}|^{2})}{(1-|V_{L4q}|^{2})^{2}-4|Q_{q}|\bar{s}^{2}_{q}(1-|V_{L4q}|^{2})+8Q_{q}^{2}\bar{s}^{4}_{q}}

(382)

where
\bar{s}_{f}^{2}
are the effective weak angles which take into account EW radiative corrections.
Then, the mixing
|V_{L4q}|
with the isosinglet quark makes the prediction for
A_{q}
lower than
the SM one.
The most precise result for quarks regards the asymmetry for b-quark final state.
Taking the data from Particle Data Group
PDG18
(also listed in table
5
),
in principle the mixing
|V_{L4b}|
modifies the couplings in the “right” direction
with respect to the experimental determinations of both
A_{b}
and
A_{FB}^{(0,b)}
.
However, considering the other constraints from
Z
decays,
a mixing
|V_{L4b}|\lesssim 0.03
(
376
) would only give a relative change to

A_{b}
at most of
\sim 0.016\%
,
two orders of magnitude less than the relative experimental error.

,
