|V_{L4d}|<0.041

Table 6:
Limits on the mixing of the SM three families with a fourth
down-type vector-like isosinglet. As in eq. (
4
),
(\delta^{d}_{Li}-\delta^{d}_{Lj})=\delta^{d}_{Lij}
.
Regarding the elements of CKM matrix,
V_{ub^{\prime}}\approx-V_{L4d}^{*}
,

V_{cb^{\prime}}\approx-V_{L4d}^{*}V_{cd}-V_{L4s}^{*}V_{cs}-V_{L4b}^{*}V_{cb}
,

V_{tb^{\prime}}\approx-V_{L4b}^{*}
as in eqs (
216
), (
217
), (
218
).
For details see the text.

Figure 13:

Upper limits obtained from kaon decays and neutral kaon mixing
on the product
|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|
of the
elements of the mixing matrix
V_{L}^{(d)}
(
4
),
as a function of their relative phase
\delta^{d}_{L21}
.

Figure 14:
Constraints on
|V_{cb^{\prime}}|
and
|V_{L4s}|
.
On the left,

|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{L4d}|=0.03
is set as a benchmark value.
The red area is excluded by the constraints on

|V_{cb^{\prime}}|
from
D^{0}
systems, with
M_{b^{\prime}}=1
TeV, as reported in table
6
.
The red lines show where the boundary shifts
if the mass of the extra quark is taken as
M_{b^{\prime}}=2
and
3
TeV.
The blue area is excluded by flavour changing processes involving K-mesons,
using the result
|V_{L4s}|<3.7\cdot 10^{-4}
, from figure
13
, eq. (
384
),
which is valid for
0\lesssim\delta^{d}_{L21}\lesssim 1.82
,

4.46\lesssim\delta^{d}_{L21}\lesssim 2\pi
.
The green region is excluded by the relation in eq. (
4.5
), for the considered values of the relative phase
\delta^{d}_{L21}
.
On the right, the same constraints are shown setting
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{L4d}|=0.04
.
The constraint on
|V_{cb^{\prime}}|
from D-mesons mixing (red area) is lowered to the red line
taking the mass of the extra quark as
M_{b^{\prime}}=1.5
TeV.

As illustrated in section
2
, the analysis
of the latest determinations of
V_{us}
obtained from kaon decays
and the ones of
V_{ud}
from beta decays results in a deviation from unitarity in the first row of the CKM matrix.
The SM unitarity relation (
8
) can be modified into
the relation in eq. (
162
) if an extra vector-like down-type quark
d_{L4},d_{R4}
participates in the mixing
of the SM families. In section
4
it was shown that a quite large mixing with the first
family,
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{L4d}|\approx 0.038(5)
(
163
),
is needed in order to explain the data
(see also table
2
,
where in this case
\delta_{\text{CKM}}=|V_{ub^{\prime}}|^{2}
).
Then, we need to verify if such large mixing is compatible with experimental constraints from flavour changing decays and electroweak observables.

In table
6
the relevant constraints extracted in this section are listed.

Limits from flavour changing kaon decays are summarized in figure
13
,
where
V_{L4d}
and
V_{L4s}
are elements of the mixing matrix
V_{L}^{(d)}
(
4
).
It results that, depending on the relative phase, the product
|V_{L4d}V_{L4s}^{*}|

cannot exceed the limit:

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}V_{L4s}^{*}|\lesssim(0.3\div 1.7)\times 10^{-5}

(383)

taking

M_{b^{\prime}}=1
TeV. Using the parameterization in eqs. (
4
), (
211
),
the maximum and minimum values are obtained for
\delta^{d}_{L21}\approx 5.0
and
\delta^{d}_{L21}=\pi
respectively.
Taking into account the needed values to recover the unitarity of the CKM matrix
in eq. (
163
),
we use
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{L4d}|=0.03
as a conservative benchmark value.
Then, from eq. (
383
) we have:

\displaystyle|V_{L4s}|<(0.9\div 5.8)\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{ub^{\prime}}|}\right]

(384)

Constraints on the value of
|V_{tb^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{L4b}|
are obtained from
flavour changing B-mesons decays, in particular from the decay
B^{0}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}

(
365
):

\displaystyle|V_{L4b}^{*}V_{L4d}|<(0.4\div 2.2)\times 10^{-4}\,,\qquad\quad|V_{L4b}|<(1.4\div 7.4)\times 10^{-3}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{ub^{\prime}}|}\right]

(385)

depending on the relative phase
\delta^{d}_{L31}
, where the maximum and
minimum value are obtained respectively for
\delta^{d}_{L31}=2.72
and

\delta^{d}_{L31}=-0.42
. Then, taking into account that (
211
), (
213
):

\displaystyle V_{L4d}\approx-\frac{h_{d1}^{*}v_{w}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}\;,\qquad V_{L4s}\approx-\frac{h_{d2}^{*}v_{w}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}\;,\qquad V_{L4b}\approx-\frac{h_{d3}^{*}v_{w}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}

(386)

where
h_{di}
are the Yukawa couplings defined in eq. (
122
),
from eqs. (
384
), (
385
) it follows that
the Yukawa couplings
h_{d2}
and
h_{d3}
should be respectively
at least
50
times and
4
times smaller than the coupling of the first family
h_{d1}
.

As regards the new column of the enlarged CKM matrix (
4
),
from eq. (
217
) we have:

\displaystyle|V_{cb^{\prime}}|\approx

\displaystyle\,|-V_{L4d}^{*}V_{cd}-V_{L4s}^{*}V_{cs}-V_{L4b}^{*}V_{cb}|=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\Big||V_{cd}||V_{ub^{\prime}}|-|V_{L4s}|e^{i\delta^{d}_{L21}}-|V_{cb}||V_{L4b}|e^{i\delta^{d}_{L31}}\Big|

(387)

Then, taking into account the constraints (
384
), (
385
), it is obtained:

\displaystyle|V_{cb^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{cd}||V_{ub^{\prime}}|

(388)

This means that, considering the constraint from
D^{0}
mesons mixing (
336
):

\displaystyle|V_{ub^{\prime}}V_{cb^{\prime}}^{*}|<4.6\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}}{1.2\cdot 10^{-14}}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}\right]

(389)

it should be that:

\displaystyle|V_{ub^{\prime}}|<0.046\left[\frac{\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}}{1.2\cdot 10^{-14}}\right]^{1/4}\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}\right]^{1/2}

(390)

In fact,
there is no much room in the parameter space to accommodate the relation (
4.5
) without contradicting experimental constraints
from
D^{0}
mixing and flavour changing kaon decays.
For
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\approx 0.03
and
M_{b^{\prime}}\approx 1
TeV, we have:

\displaystyle|V_{cb^{\prime}}|\approx(6.6\pm 0.4)\times 10^{-3}\,,\qquad|V_{tb^{\prime}}|<(1.4\div 7.4)\times 10^{-3}

(391)

The first condition is shown in figure
14
. There, the blue area is excluded by flavour changing processes involving K-mesons (
384
).
We use the upper limit obtained for
\delta^{d}_{L21}=\text{Arg}(V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d})=0
,

|V_{L4s}^{*}V_{L4d}|<1.1\times 10^{-5}
, which is valid for

0<\delta^{d}_{L21}<1.82
,
4.46<\delta^{d}_{L21}<2\pi

(for other values of the phase
\delta^{d}_{L21}
the constraint should be more stringent,
see figure
13
).
The green region is excluded by the relation in eq. (
4.5
),
for any value of the relative phase
\delta^{d}_{L31}

and for the considered values of the relative phase
\delta^{d}_{L21}

(other values of
\delta^{d}_{L21}
only allow a slightly larger opening angle of the allowed
“cone” on the upper side, which does not widen the allowed range of values of
V_{cb^{\prime}}
,
because the constraint from kaon physics is more stringent for those other values of the phase
\delta^{d}_{L21}
).
The red area is excluded by the constraints on

|V_{cb^{\prime}}|
from
D^{0}
systems (
336
) with
M_{b^{\prime}}=1
TeV:

\displaystyle|V_{cb^{\prime}}|<1.5\cdot 10^{-2}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{ub^{\prime}}|}\right]\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{b^{\prime}}}\right]

(392)

As discussed in section
4.2.3
, the constraint is obtained by allowing the new contribution to the mass difference in

D^{0}
mesons system
to account for the experimental value
\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}
, at
95\%
C.L..
This means that the extra singlet quark can be thought as a way to
explain the value of the mass difference in neutral D-mesons system.

As can be seen, a narrow allowed region (including
V_{L4s}=0
) can be found.
However, the mass of the extra quark cannot exceed few TeV.
In fact, with larger values of
M_{b^{\prime}}

the constraint from
D^{0}
mixing becomes more stringent.
For example,
as shown in figure
14
, the allowed region vanishes if
M_{b^{\prime}}=3
TeV
with the choice
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|=0.03
,
while
M_{b^{\prime}}=1.5
TeV is large enough to make the
allowed area disappear if
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|=0.04
is needed.
Also the constraint from flavour changing processes of kaons
shifts towards lower values with larger values of the extra quark mass.
Moreover, with
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|=0.03
,
M_{b^{\prime}}<5.8\,\text{TeV}
is needed
for the perturbativity (
|h_{d\,1}|<1
).
Anyway, also by taking
M_{b^{\prime}}=1
TeV, the allowed parameter space vanishes with

|V_{ub^{\prime}}|>0.046
.

In addition to that, results on
Z
-boson decay rate into hadrons imply that
(
381
):

\displaystyle|V_{L4d}|^{2}<1.7\times 10^{-3}\;,\qquad\quad|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\approx|V_{L4d}|<0.041

(393)

which is in the range of values needed to solve the CKM unitarity problem
(for example, at
95\%
C.L.
|V_{L4d}|=0.038^{+0.008}_{-0.011}
(
163
)
using our conservative average for
V_{ud}
(
15
)).
This means that an extra weak singlet would not be a good solution of the CKM
unitarity problem with the more extreme values of the determinations of CKM elements,
as can also be seen by comparing eq. (
381
)
to the needed values
|V_{L4d}|^{2}=\delta_{\text{CKM}}

displayed in table
2
.

From eq. (
391
) we can notice that the mixing of

b^{\prime}
with c-quark and t-quark should be at least four times smaller
than the mixing with the u-quark.
Moreover,
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|\sim 0.03
is comparable to
|V_{cb}|

and ten times larger than
|V_{ub}|
.
However, although it may seem unnatural to expect
a larger mixing of the 4th state with the lightest family than with the heavier ones,
it cannot be excluded.

5
Extra up-type isosinglet

The case of the addition of a up-type vector-like isosinglet couple of quarks

(u_{4L},u_{4R})
is examined in this section.
Analogously to the case of the extra down-type quark,
there is an additional piece in the Yukawa Lagrangian:

\displaystyle y^{u}_{ij}\tilde{\varphi}\overline{q_{Li}}u_{Rj}+y^{d}_{ij}\varphi\overline{q_{Li}}d_{Rj}+h_{ui}\tilde{\varphi}\overline{q_{Li}}u_{R4}+M_{4u}\overline{u_{L4}}u_{R4}+\text{h.c.}

(394)

with
i,j=1,2,3
and

\tilde{\varphi}=i\tau_{2}\varphi^{*}
. Then the up-type quarks mass matrix looks like:

\displaystyle\overline{u_{Li}}\mathbf{m}^{(u)}_{ij}u_{Rj}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{u_{L1}},\overline{u_{L2}},\overline{u_{L3}},\overline{u_{L4}})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc|c}&&&h_{u1}v_{w}\\
&\mathbf{y}^{(u)}_{3\times 3}v_{w}&&h_{u2}v_{w}\\
&&&h_{u3}v_{w}\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0&M_{4u}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{R1}\\
u_{R2}\\
u_{R3}\\
u_{R4}\end{array}\right)+\text{h.c.}

where
v_{w}=174
GeV is the SM Higgs vacuum expectation value (VEV)
and
\mathbf{y}^{(u)}_{3\times 3}
is the
3\times 3
matrix of Yukawa couplings.
The mass matrix
\mathbf{m}^{(u)}
can be diagonalized with positive eigenvalues by a biunitary transformation:

\displaystyle V_{L}^{(u)\dagger}\mathbf{m}^{(u)}V_{R}^{(u)}=\mathbf{m}^{(u)}_{\text{diag}}=\text{diag}(y_{u}v_{w},y_{c}v_{w},y_{t}v_{w},M_{t^{\prime}})

(403)

where
V^{(u)}_{L,R}
are two unitary
4\times 4
matrices.
\mathbf{m}^{(u)}_{\text{diag}}

is the diagonal matrix of mass eigenvalues
m_{u,c,t}=y_{u,c,t}v_{w}

and
M_{t^{\prime}}\approx M_{4u}
.
The mass eigenstates are:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L}\\
c_{L}\\
t_{L}\\
t^{\prime}_{L}\end{array}\right)=V_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L1}\\
u_{L2}\\
u_{L3}\\
u_{L4}\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad V^{(u)}_{L}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{L1u}&V_{L1c}&V_{L1t}&V_{L1t^{\prime}}\\
V_{L2u}&V_{L2c}&V_{L2t}&V_{L2t^{\prime}}\\
V_{L3u}&V_{L3c}&V_{L3t}&V_{L3t^{\prime}}\\
V_{L4u}&V_{L4c}&V_{L4t}&V_{L4t^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)

while the three up-type quarks involved in charged weak interactions expressed in terms of mass
eigenstates are:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L1}\\
u_{L2}\\
u_{L3}\end{array}\right)=\tilde{V}^{(u)}_{L}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L}\\
c_{L}\\
t_{L}\\
t^{\prime}_{L}\end{array}\right)_{L}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{L1u}&V_{L1c}&V_{L1t}&V_{L1t^{\prime}}\\
V_{L2u}&V_{L2c}&V_{L2t}&V_{L2t^{\prime}}\\
V_{L3u}&V_{L3c}&V_{L3t}&V_{L3t^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L}\\
c_{L}\\
t_{L}\\
t^{\prime}_{L}\end{array}\right)

where
\tilde{V}^{(u)}_{L}
is the
3\times 4
submatrix of
V^{(u)}_{L}
without the last row.
Although it still holds that
\tilde{V}^{(u)}_{L}\tilde{V}^{(u){\dagger}}_{L}=1_{3\times 3}
(
1_{3\times 3}
being the
3\times 3
identity matrix),

\tilde{V}^{(u)}_{L}
is not unitary
\tilde{V}^{(u){\dagger}}_{L}\tilde{V}^{(u)}_{L}\neq 1_{3\times 3}
.

The Lagrangian for the charged current interaction is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{cc}}

\displaystyle=\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overline{u_{L1}}&\overline{u_{L2}}&\overline{u_{L3}}\end{array}\right)\gamma^{\mu}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{L1}\\
d_{L2}\\
d_{L3}\end{array}\right)W_{\mu}^{+}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\overline{u_{L}}&\overline{c_{L}}&\overline{t_{L}}&\overline{t^{\prime}_{L}}\end{array}\right)\gamma^{\mu}\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{L}\\
s_{L}\\
b_{L}\end{array}\right)W_{\mu}^{+}+\text{h.c.}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}=\tilde{V}^{(u){\dagger}}_{L}V_{L}^{(d)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}V_{ud}&V_{us}&V_{ub}\\
V_{cd}&V_{cs}&V_{cb}\\
V_{td}&V_{ts}&V_{tb}\\
V_{t^{\prime}d}&V_{t^{\prime}s}&V_{t^{\prime}b}\end{array}\right)

is a
4\times 3
matrix,
V^{(d)}_{L}
being the unitary
3\times 3
matrix diagonalizing the
down-type quark mass matrix from the left.
Again
\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}
is not unitary:

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}^{\dagger}=\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u)}\neq\mathbf{1}

(442)

In particular, for the first row it holds that

\displaystyle|V_{ud}|^{2}+|V_{us}|^{2}+|V_{ub}|^{2}=[\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}^{\dagger}]_{11}=[\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u)}]_{11}=1-|V_{L4u}|^{2}

(443)

which is the same extended unitarity condition as in eqs. (
9
) and (
162
),
where
\delta_{\text{CKM}}=|V_{L4u}|^{2}
and
|V_{L4u}|
has
the same effect on the unitarity of the first row as
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|
.
Then, the analysis
of the determinations of
V_{us}
obtained from
leptonic and semileptonic kaon decays
and of
V_{ud}
from beta decays gives the same result
as in section
4
, leading to the best fit point in eq. (
163
):

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}|^{2}=1.48(36)\times 10^{-3}\;,\qquad\quad|V_{L4u}|=0.038(5)

(444)

which shifts the values of
V_{us}
obtained from the three determinations
as shown in figure
5
.

The mixing matrix
V^{(u)}_{L}
induces non-standard couplings of
Z
-boson with the LH up quarks,
since
u_{L4}
is a weak singlet while the first three (SM) families act as weak doublets.
In fact,
Z
-boson couples as
Z_{\mu}\overline{f}(T_{3}+Q\sin^{2}\theta_{W})f
, where
T_{3}
is the weak isospin projection and
Q
the electric charge. Therefore,
Q
-dependent couplings remain diagonal between the mass eigenstates
u,,t,t^{\prime}
, while the
T_{3}
dependent part gets non-diagonal couplings.
The weak neutral current Lagrangian for up quarks reads:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{nc}}=\frac{g}{\cos\theta_{W}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overline{u_{L1}}&\overline{u_{L2}}&\overline{u_{L3}}\end{array}\right)\gamma^{\mu}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L1}\\
u_{L2}\\
u_{L3}\end{array}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\left(\overline{\mathbf{u}_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{u}_{L}+\overline{\mathbf{u}_{R}}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{u}_{R}\right)\right]Z_{\mu}=

\displaystyle=\frac{g}{\cos\theta_{W}}\!\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\overline{u_{L}}&\overline{c_{L}}&\overline{t_{L}}&\overline{t^{\prime}_{L}}\end{array}\right)\gamma^{\mu}\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u)}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L}\\
c_{L}\\
t_{L}\\
t^{\prime}_{L}\end{array}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\left(\overline{\mathbf{u}_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{u}_{L}+\overline{\mathbf{u}_{R}}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{u}_{R}\right)\right]Z_{\mu}

where
\mathbf{u}
is the column vector of the four up-type quarks
u,c,t,t^{\prime}
.
Then, as shown in eq. (
5
), the non-unitarity of
\tilde{V}^{(u)}_{L}
is at the origin of
non-diagonal couplings with
Z
boson, which means
flavor changing neutral currents (FCNC)
at tree level. Explicitly, the weak isospin dependent part of the
Z
coupling is given by:

\displaystyle V_{\text{nc}}^{(u)}=\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u)}=V^{(u){\dagger}}_{L}\text{diag}(1,1,1,0)V^{(u)}_{L}=

\displaystyle=\!\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{1\tabcolsep}}c@{\hspace{1\tabcolsep}}c@{\hspace{1\tabcolsep}}c}1\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\\
0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&1\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\\
0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&1\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\\
0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&0\end{array}\right)\!+\!\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{1\tabcolsep}}c@{\hspace{1\tabcolsep}}c@{\hspace{1.5\tabcolsep}}c}-|V_{L4u}|^{2}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4t}\hfil\hskip 9.0pt&-V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4t^{\prime}}\\
-V_{L4c}^{*}V_{L4u}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-|V_{L4c}|^{2}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-V_{L4c}^{*}V_{L4t}\hfil\hskip 9.0pt&-V_{L4c}^{*}V_{L4t^{\prime}}\\
-V_{L4t}^{*}V_{L4u}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-V_{L4t}^{*}V_{L4c}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-|V_{L4t}|^{2}\hfil\hskip 9.0pt&-V_{L4t}^{*}V_{L4t^{\prime}}\\
-V_{L4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{L4u}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-V_{L4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{L4c}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&-V_{L4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{L4t}\hfil\hskip 9.0pt&|V_{L1t^{\prime}}|^{2}\!+\!|V_{L2t^{\prime}}|^{2}\!+\!|V_{L3t^{\prime}}|^{2}\end{array}\right)

V^{(u)}_{L}
can be parameterized analogously to eqs. (
4
), (
4
):

\displaystyle V_{L}^{(u)}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}&&&0\\
&V_{3\times 3}^{(u)}&&0\\
&&&0\\
0&0&0&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}c_{L1}^{u}&-\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u}\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u*}&-\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u*}\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}&\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u*}\\
0&c_{L2}^{u}&-\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u*}\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}&\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u*}\\
0&0&c_{L3}^{u}&\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u*}\\
-\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u}&-\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u}&-\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}&c_{L1}^{u}c_{L2}^{u}c_{L3}^{u}\end{array}\right)

Then the elements of the last row correspond to:

\displaystyle V_{L4u}=-\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u}\,,

\displaystyle V_{L4c}\approx-\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u}\,,

\displaystyle V_{L4t}\approx-\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}

(470)

c^{u}_{Li}
are cosines and
\tilde{s}^{u}_{Li}
are complex sines
(one of which can be made real) of angles in the
1\,4
,
2\,4
,
3\,4
family
planes parameterizing the mixing of the first three families with the vector-like species:

\displaystyle\tilde{s}^{u}_{Li}=\sin\theta^{u}_{Li4}e^{i\delta^{u}_{Li}}=s^{u}_{Li}e^{i\delta^{u}_{Li}}\;,\qquad\quad c^{u}_{Li}=\cos\theta^{u}_{Li4}

\displaystyle\delta^{u}_{Lij}=\delta^{u}_{Li}-\delta^{u}_{Lj}

(471)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{Li}^{u}\approx\frac{h^{*}_{ui}v_{w}}{M_{t^{\prime}}}

(472)

where we recalled
h_{ui}
the couplings after the diagonalization

V^{(u)}_{Lij}h_{uj}\approx h_{ui}
.

V^{(d)}_{L}
can be naturally chosen with small mixings, so that, in the parameterization
of eq. (
5
),

V_{3\times 3}^{(u)}
is basically equal to the
3\times 3
submatrix of
\tilde{V}_{L}^{(u)}

and diagonalizes the
3\times 3
submatrix
\mathbf{y}^{(u)}_{3\times 3}
in eq. (
5
).
Moreover,
as it will be shown, in order to have
V_{L4u}\approx 0.03
it should be

|V_{L4c}|<5\times 10^{-3}
,

|V_{t^{\prime}b}|<8.5\times 10^{-2}
.
Then,
\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}
can be parameterized as:

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}V_{ud}&V_{us}&V_{ub}\\
V_{cd}&V_{cs}&V_{cb}\\
V_{td}&V_{ts}&V_{tb}\\
V_{t^{\prime}d}&V_{t^{\prime}s}&V_{t^{\prime}b}\end{array}\right)\simeq\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}c_{L1}^{u}&0&0\\
-\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u*}\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u}&c_{L2}^{u}&0\\
-\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u}\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u*}&-\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u}\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u*}&c_{L3}^{u}\\
\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u}&\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u}&\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}\end{array}\right)\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}V_{ud}&V_{us}&V_{ub}\\
V_{cd}&V_{cs}&V_{cb}\\
V^{\prime}_{td}&V_{ts}&V_{tb}\end{array}\right)\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}V_{ud}&V_{us}&V_{ub}\\
V_{cd}&V_{cs}&V_{cb}\\
V_{td}&V_{ts}&V_{tb}\\
\tilde{s}_{L1}^{u}&\;\tilde{s}^{u}_{L1}V_{us}+\tilde{s}_{L2}^{u}V_{cs}+\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}V_{ts}&\tilde{s}_{L3}^{u}\end{array}\right)

where in this parameterization the
3\times 3
submatrix (made of the first three rows)
contains

3
angles and one phase. Also another phase can be absorbed,
so we can always choose
\delta^{u}_{L1}=0
without loss of generality.
Hence, for
the elements of the fourth row of
\tilde{V}_{\text{CKM}}
in (
5
)
it holds that:

\displaystyle V_{t^{\prime}d}\approx-V_{L4u}

\displaystyle V_{t^{\prime}s}\approx-V_{L4u}V_{us}-V_{L4c}V_{cs}-V_{L4t}V_{ts}

(488)

\displaystyle V_{t^{\prime}b}\approx-V_{L4t}

Also the couplings of quarks with the real Higgs are not diagonal
in presence of the vector-like up-quark.
In fact, the matrix of Yukawa couplings and the mass matrix (
5
) are not proportional anymore,
and then they are not diagonalized by the same transformation.
In particular, similarly to eq. (
4
),
left-handed SM quarks are coupled with
t^{\prime}_{R}
with coupling constants which can be in principle
of order
O(1)
:

\displaystyle(\overline{u_{1L}},\overline{u_{2L}},\overline{u_{3L}},\overline{u_{4L}})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc|c}&&&h_{u1}\\
&\mathbf{y}^{(u)}_{3\times 3}&&h_{u2}\\
&&&h_{u3}\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{1}\\
u_{2}\\
u_{3}\\
u_{4}\end{array}\right)_{R}\frac{H^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}+\text{h.c.}\approx

\displaystyle(\overline{u_{L}},\overline{c_{L}},\overline{t_{L}},\overline{t^{\prime}_{L}})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc c}y_{1}^{u}&0&0&h_{u1}\\
0&y^{u}_{2}&0&h_{u2}\\
0&0&y^{u}_{3}&h_{u3}\\
0&0&0&\sum_{i=1}^{3}(\tilde{s}_{Li}^{u}h_{ui})\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{R}\\
c_{R}\\
t_{R}\\
t^{\prime}_{R}\end{array}\right)\frac{H^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}+\text{h.c.}

Let us also notice that,
because of the large mixing with the first family, the extra quark
t^{\prime}
would mainly decay
into
u
or
d
quark via the couplings with
W
,
Z
,
H
.
The
CMS experiment put a lower limit
M_{t^{\prime}}\gtrsim 685
GeV
CMS
, which
implies that

|V_{t^{\prime}d}|\simeq 0.03

can be obtained if
|h_{u1}|\gtrsim 0.1
, much larger than the Yukawa constant of the bottom quark.
In turn, by taking
|V_{L4u}|>0.03
in

M_{t^{\prime}}=|h_{u1}|v_{w}/|V_{t^{\prime}d}|
,
and assuming (for the perturbativity)
h_{u1}\lesssim 1
,
there is an upper limit on the extra quark mass,
M_{t^{\prime}}<5.8
TeV.

In the following sections experimental limits from FCNC and electroweak observables are examined.
The results are summarized in section
5.4
, in table
7
and figures
16
,
18
.

5.1
Limits from rare
D
mesons decays

Mixings of the standard quarks
with the additional vector-like singlet up-quark
t^{\prime}

should be constrained by D-mesons rare decays.
In fact, the non-diagonal couplings of
Z
-boson with light quarks
in eqs. (
5
) induce at tree level flavour changing leptonic and semileptonic decays
of D-mesons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{new}}=\frac{4G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}c_{L})\left[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})(\overline{\ell_{L}}\gamma_{\mu}\ell_{L})+\sin^{2}\theta_{W}(\overline{\ell_{R}}\gamma_{\mu}\ell_{R})\right]+\text{h.c.}

(505)

The most stringent constraint from semileptonic decays comes from the experimental limit
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(D^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}<7.3\cdot 10^{-8}\qquad 90\%\,\text{C.L.}

(506)

Neglecting the SM contribution, we can impose the experimental limit on the new contribution:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(D^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{+}\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{new}}

\displaystyle\simeq\text{Br}(D^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\mu^{+}\nu_{\mu})\frac{2|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|^{2}[(-\frac{1}{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{W})^{2}+\sin^{4}\theta_{W}]}{|V_{cd}|^{2}}

\displaystyle<7.3\cdot 10^{-8}

(507)

from which

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|<2.0\cdot 10^{-3}\left[\frac{\text{Br}_{\text{max}}}{7.3\cdot 10^{-8}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(508)

where
we take advantage of the experimental branching ratio

\text{Br}(D^{+}\rightarrow\pi^{0}\mu^{+}\nu_{\mu})=(3.50\pm 0.15)\cdot 10^{-3}

PDG18
.

\text{Br}_{\text{max}}
is defined as in eq. (
250
) as the chosen maximum value
allowed for the rate which would be generated by the beyond SM amplitude alone.

The experimental constraint on the leptonic decay
D^{0}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-}

is
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(D^{0}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{exp}}<6.2\cdot 10^{-9}\qquad 90\%\,\text{C.L.}

(509)

Neglecting the SM short- and long- distance contribution, we can limit the new contribution obtained from the operator (
505
):

\displaystyle\text{Br}(D^{0}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})_{\text{new}}=\text{Br}(D^{+}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\nu_{\mu})\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tau(D^{0})M_{D^{0}}\sqrt{1-4\frac{m^{2}_{\mu}}{M^{2}_{D^{0}}}}}{\tau(D^{+})M_{D^{+}}\left(1-\frac{m^{2}_{\mu}}{M^{2}_{D^{+}}}\right)^{2}}\frac{|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|^{2}}{|V_{cd}|^{2}}<6.2\cdot 10^{-9}

(510)

It is obtained that:

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|<2.0\cdot 10^{-3}\left[\frac{\text{Br}_{\text{max}}}{6.2\cdot 10^{-9}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(511)

using the values in table
4
, with

\text{Br}(D^{+}\rightarrow\mu^{+}\nu_{\mu})=(3.74\pm 0.17)\cdot 10^{-4}
.

\text{Br}_{\text{max}}
is the maximum value
allowed for the rate which would be generated by the beyond SM amplitude alone.

5.2
Limits from neutral mesons systems

5.2.1
D^{0}
-
\bar{D}^{0}
mixing

As described in Section
4.2.3
, the experimental result (
332
) for the mass difference
in the
D^{0}
system
allows values
which can be
two orders of magnitude higher than the SM short-distance expectation,
while long-distance effects cannot be computed reliably.
Then,
new physics scenarios
can in principle account for the experimental mass difference

\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}
in eq. (
332
).

The non-diagonal couplings of Higgs and
Z
bosons with quarks
in eqs. (
5
), (
5
)
bring additional contributions
to the transition
\bar{D}^{0}\leftrightarrow D^{0}
, both at tree level and loop level.
Analogously to eq. (
4.2.1
), the corresponding effective Lagrangian reads:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta C=2}

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}(V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c})^{2}\left(1+\frac{G_{F}M^{2}_{t^{\prime}}}{8\sqrt{2}\pi^{2}}\right)(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}c_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}(V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c})^{2}f(M_{t^{\prime}})(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}c_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c.}

(512)

\displaystyle f(M_{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=1+\left(\frac{M_{t^{\prime}}}{3.1\,\text{TeV}}\right)^{2}

(513)

Hence, analogously to the kaons case, the additional contribution to the mixing mass is:

\displaystyle 2m_{D^{0}}M_{12\,\text{new}}^{D*}=-\langle\bar{D}^{0}|\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta C=2}|D^{0}\rangle

(514)

where in VIA approximation:

\displaystyle\langle\bar{D}^{0}|(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}c_{L})^{2}|D^{0}\rangle=\frac{2}{3}f^{2}_{D}m^{2}_{D^{0}}\,.

(515)

Then,
the new operator (
5.2.1
)
can generate the mass difference:

\displaystyle\Delta m_{D\,\text{new}}\approx 2|M_{12\,\text{new}}^{D}|\simeq\frac{2}{3}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}f(M_{t^{\prime}})|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|^{2}f^{2}_{D}m_{D^{0}}

(516)

We can assume that
\Delta m_{D\,\text{new}}
is the dominant contribution to the mass difference.
Then,
by using the
95\%
C.L. limit obtained from eq. (
332
) we have:

\displaystyle\Delta m_{D\,\text{new}}<1.2\times 10^{-14}

(517)

leading to:

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|<1.5\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{f(1\,\text{TeV})}{f(M_{t^{\prime}})}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{\Delta m_{D\text{max}}}{1.2\cdot 10^{-14}}\right]

(518)

5.2.2
K^{0}
-
\bar{K}^{0}
mixing

Figure 15:
New contribution to
\bar{K}^{0}\rightarrow K^{0}
mixing,
q=u,c,t,t^{\prime}
.

In the SM, the short-distance contribution to the transition

K^{0}(d\bar{s})\leftrightarrow\bar{K}^{0}(\bar{d}s)

arises from weak box diagrams (figure
8
), which corresponds to the
effective Lagrangian in eq. (
288
).

Figure 16:

Upper limits on the product
|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|
from neutral kaon mixing,
obtained from the constraint in eqs. eqs. (
521
), (
524
)
with
\Delta_{K}=1
,
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}=0.4
,
as a function of the relative phase
\delta_{ds}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})
.
The blue and orange curves indicate where the upper limit shifts for
M_{t^{\prime}}=5
TeV.

In the scenario with extra up-type quark, the mixing of the SM quarks with the
t^{\prime}
-quark
in the charged current in eq. (
5
) gives additional contributions to the
K^{0}
mixing,
in interference with the SM.
In fact, the same operator as in (
288
) is originated by box diagrams with internal

t^{\prime}
-quark running in the loop, as shown in figure
15
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta S=2}=-\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{4\pi^{2}}

\displaystyle\Big((V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})^{2}S(x_{t^{\prime}})+2(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})S(x_{c},x_{t^{\prime}})+

\displaystyle\quad+2(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})S(x_{t},x_{t^{\prime}})\Big)(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})(\overline{s_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})+\text{h.c.}

(519)

where
x_{a}=\frac{m_{a}^{2}}{m_{W}^{2}}
and

S_{0}(x,y)
are the Inami-Lim functions
Inami:1980fz
in eqs. (
289
), (
290
).

As described in section
4.2.1
,
the mass splitting
\Delta m_{K}=m_{K_{L}}-m_{K_{S}}

and the CP-violating parameter
\epsilon_{K}
are dominated by the short distance contribution, which is given by
the off-diagonal term
M_{12}
of the mass matrix of neutral kaons:

\displaystyle M_{12}=-\frac{1}{2m_{K^{0}}}\langle K^{0}|\mathcal{L}_{\Delta S=2}|\bar{K}^{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\Delta m_{K}\approx 2|M_{12}|+\Delta m_{\text{LD}}

\displaystyle|\epsilon_{K}|\approx\frac{|\text{Im}[M_{12}]|}{\sqrt{2}\Delta m_{K}}

(520)

where
\Delta m_{\text{LD}}
is the unknown long distance contribution.
Then, in order to not contradict experimental results,
the new contribution (
519
), and in particular the mixing elements

V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}
, should be constrained.

The mixing mass in the SM
M_{12}^{\text{SM}}
is given in eq. (
295
).
Regarding the CP-conserving part, as in section
4.2.1
,
in order to estimate the constraint on the new mixing elements
we can impose:

\displaystyle\left|M_{12}^{\text{new}}\right|<\left|M_{12}^{\text{SM}}\right|\,\Delta_{K}

(521)

where
\Delta_{K}
is real and positive and we will evaluate for
\Delta_{K}=1
.
This is analogous to comparing the coefficients of the effective operators in
eqs. (
288
) and (
519
):

\displaystyle\Big|(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})^{2}S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})+2(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t^{\prime}})+

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})S_{0}(x_{t},x_{t^{\prime}})\Big|<S_{0}(x_{c})|V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd}|^{2}\,\Delta_{K}

(522)

In this case the upper bound
is basically determined by constraining the contribution of
the box diagram with
t^{\prime}
running in the loop:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|^{2}S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})<S_{0}(x_{c})(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})^{2}\,\Delta_{K}

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|<5.2\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{t^{\prime}}}\right]\left[\Delta_{K}\right]^{1/2}

(523)

The scaling with
t^{\prime}
mass in eq. (
523
) holds since
S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})\sim\frac{1}{4}x_{t^{\prime}}
for
M_{t^{\prime}}\gtrsim 2
TeV.

Regarding the contribution to the CP-violating parameter
\epsilon_{K}
,
we can estimate the upper bound on the new operator by constraining
the imaginary part of the new contribution to the mixing mass
|\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{new}}|

to be a fraction
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}
of the SM contribution,
as in eq. (
312
):

\displaystyle|\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{new}}|<|\text{Im}M_{12}^{\text{SM}}|\,\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}

(524)

At leading order of the new physics contribution, eq. (
524
) is equivalent to comparing the magnitude of the imaginary part of operators
(
288
) and (
519
), in the standard parameterization of CKM matrix:

\displaystyle\left|\text{\large{Im}}\Big((V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})^{2}S(x_{t^{\prime}})+2(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t^{\prime}})+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 142.26378pt+2(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})S_{0}(x_{t},x_{t^{\prime}})\Big)\right|<\left|\text{\large{Im}}(\mathcal{C}_{\text{SM}})\right|\,\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}

(525)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{SM}}=\eta_{1}(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})^{2}S_{0}(x_{c})+\eta_{2}(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})^{2}S_{0}(x_{t})+2\eta_{3}(V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd})(V_{ts}^{*}V_{td})S_{0}(x_{c},x_{t})

(526)

This constraint also depends on the mass of the extra quark,
since the first term of eq. (
519
) scales as

S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})\sim\frac{1}{4}M_{t^{\prime}}^{2}/m_{W}^{2}
for
M_{t^{\prime}}\gtrsim 2
TeV.
while the second and third components are logarithmically growing with
t^{\prime}
mass.
We make an estimation choosing
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}=0.4
,
corresponding to an equivalent scale of the new operator

\Lambda_{ds,\text{Im}}=2.5\cdot 10^{4}\,\text{TeV}
, as defined in eq. (
314
).
The upper limit on the product
V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}

obtained from the conditions in eqs. (
5.2.2
), (
525
)
with
\Delta_{K}=1
,
\Delta_{\epsilon_{K}}=0.4

is shown in figure
16
,
where we used the Wolfenstein parameterization as in eq. (
245
) and
\delta_{ds}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})
.
The excluded region is shown for
M_{t^{\prime}}=1
TeV, while the lines show the lowered upper limit
for
M_{t^{\prime}}=6
TeV.

5.2.3
B_{s,d}^{0}
-
\bar{B}_{s,d}^{0}
mixing

As illustrated in section
4.2.2
,
in the SM the dominant contribution to
B^{0}_{d}(\bar{b}d)
-
\bar{B}_{d}^{0}(\bar{d}b)
and
B^{0}_{s}(\bar{b}s)
-
\bar{B}_{s}^{0}(\bar{s}b)

mixings comes from
box-diagrams with internal top-quark, as shown in figure
10
, with effective Lagrangian in eqs.
(
317
), (
318
),
and, analogously to the kaons system:

\displaystyle 2M_{B^{0}_{d,s}}M_{12}^{(d,s)*}=\langle\bar{B}_{d,s}^{0}|-\mathcal{L}_{\Delta B(d,s)=2}|B^{0}_{d,s}\rangle

\displaystyle\Delta M_{d,s}=2|M_{12}^{(d,s)}|

(527)

In the scenario with extra up-type quark, the mixing of the SM quarks with the
t^{\prime}
-quark
in the charged current in eq. (
5
)
contribute to neutral B-mesons mixing, in interference with the SM.
In fact, the same operator as in (
317
) is originated by box diagrams with internal

t^{\prime}
and
t,t^{\prime}
-quarks:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta B(d)=2}=-\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{4\pi^{2}}

\displaystyle\left((V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})^{2}S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})+2(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})(V_{tb}^{*}V_{td})S_{0}(x_{t},x_{t^{\prime}})\right)(\overline{b_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c. }

(528)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{new}}_{\Delta B(s)=2}=-\frac{G^{2}_{F}m^{2}_{W}}{4\pi^{2}}

\displaystyle\left((V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}s})^{2}S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})+2(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}s})(V_{tb}^{*}V_{ts})S_{0}(x_{t},x_{t^{\prime}})\right)(\overline{b_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}s_{L})^{2}+\text{h.c. }

(529)

where
x_{a}=\frac{m_{a}^{2}}{m_{W}^{2}}
and
S(x_{a})
are the Inami-Lim functions
Inami:1980fz
given in eqs. (
289
), (
290
).
The c-quark contribution is negligible.

We can constrain the new contribution to be less than a fraction
\Delta_{B_{(d,s)}}
of the SM contribution

\Delta M_{d,s}^{\text{SM}}=2|M_{12}^{(d,s)\text{SM}}|
given in eq. (
322
), as:

\displaystyle|M_{12}^{(d,s)\text{new}}|<|M_{12}^{(d,s)\text{SM}}|\,\Delta_{B_{d,s}}

(530)

This is analogous to comparing the coefficients of the effective operators in
eqs. (
528
), (
529
) with the SM ones (
317
), (
318
):

\displaystyle\Big|(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d/s})^{2}S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})+2(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d/s})(V_{tb}^{*}V_{td/s})S_{0}(x_{t},x_{t^{\prime}})\Big|<S(x_{t})|V_{tb}^{*}V_{td/s}|^{2}\,\Delta_{B_{d,s}}

(531)

where we evaluate the constraint at leading order of both SM and new physics contributions.
As in section
4.2.2
, we use
\Delta_{B_{(d,s)}}=0.3
as a benchmark value.
Then, by taking
M_{t^{\prime}}=1
TeV, we obtain
(with
m_{t}(m_{t})=163
GeV):

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|<0.4\div 2.6\times 10^{-3}

(532)

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}s}|<0.2\div 1.2\times 10^{-2}

(533)

depending on the relative phases

\delta_{db}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})
,
\delta_{sb}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}s})
.
The constraints become more stringent for higher values of
t^{\prime}
mass.
The contribution from the mixed box diagrams
with
t,t^{\prime}
grows logarithmically with
M_{t^{\prime}}
, but the
contribution from the box-diagram with
t^{\prime}
increases linearly with the mass for
M_{t^{\prime}}\gtrsim 2
TeV.
Then, for
M_{t^{\prime}}\gtrsim 3
TeV the upper limits start to decrease as
M_{t^{\prime}}^{-1}
.
The upper bound on the mixing elements
|V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|
,
|V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|
as a function of the
relative phase of the elements is shown in
figure
17
for increasing values of the extra quark
t^{\prime}
mass.

Figure 17:
Upper limits on the products of the mixing elements

|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|
,
|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|

from neutral B-mesons mixing,
obtained from the constraint in eqs. eqs. (
531
)
with
\Delta_{B_{d,s}}=0.3
,
as a function of the relative phases

\delta_{db}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})
,

,
evaluated for the values of extra quark masses

,

,

,

TeV
(blue, purple, orange and black curves respectively).

5.3
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{hadr})
,
\Gamma(Z)

The presence of vector-like quarks affect both
off-diagonal couplings and diagonal couplings
of Z-boson with quarks, changing the prediction of the observables
related to the
Z
boson physics.

The SM predictions for the Z decay rate and partial decay rate into hadrons are
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z)_{\text{SM}}=2.4942\pm 0.0009\,\text{GeV}\,,\qquad\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{hadr})_{\text{SM}}=1.7411\pm 0.0008\,\text{GeV}

(534)

to be compared with the experimental results
PDG18
:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Z)_{\text{exp}}=2.4952\pm 0.0023\,\text{GeV}\,,\qquad\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{hadr})_{\text{exp}}=1.7444\pm 0.0020\,\text{GeV}

(535)

In this BSM scenario, the
partial decay rate into hadrons
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})

(which also corresponds to the deviation of the total
Z
decay rate
\Gamma(Z)
,
since there are not additional leptons)
would be changed with respect to the SM expectation by:

\displaystyle\Gamma(\text{had})-\Gamma(\text{had})_{\text{SM}}=\Gamma(Z)-\Gamma(Z)_{\text{SM}}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left[\sum_{i,j=u,c}\left|\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}V_{Lki}^{*}V_{Lkj}-\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\delta_{ij}\right|^{2}-2\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left[\sum_{q=u,c}\left(\frac{1}{2}(1-|V_{L4q}|^{2})-\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|^{2}-2\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)^{2}\right]\approx

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}M^{3}_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{W}\right)\left(|V_{L4u}|^{2}+|V_{L4c}|^{2}\right)<0

(536)

where the QCD corrections factor
\approx 1.050
should be included.
The extra contribution to the decay rate
should be constrained:

\displaystyle|\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})-\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{SM}}|<\Delta\Gamma_{Z}

(537)

As shown in eq. (
536
), the prediction for the decay rate is lowered with respect to the SM one.
Since the SM expectation (
534
) is below the experimental result (
535
),
we can choose to use the limit value of the SM prediction

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{SM}}=1.7419
GeV in eq. (
536
).
Then we can impose that

\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})
should stay
in the
95\%
C.L. interval of the experimental value
\Gamma(Z\rightarrow\text{had})_{\text{exp}}
,
which means:

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}|^{2}+|V_{L4c}|^{2}<2.0\cdot 10^{-3}\left[\frac{\Delta\Gamma_{Z}}{1.4\times 10^{-3}\,\text{GeV}}\right]

(538)

The last constraint is satisfied if both
|V_{L4u,c}|\lesssim 0.032
. With
|V_{L4u}|=0.03
, it means
|V_{L4c}|<0.034
. With
V_{L4c}=0
, the constraint implies:

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}|^{2}<2.0\cdot 10^{-3}\;,\qquad\quad|V_{L4u}|<0.044

(539)

which is extremely close to the value needed to solve the CKM unitarity problem
(for example, at
95\%
C.L.
|V_{L4u}|=0.038^{+0.008}_{-0.011}
(
163
)
using our conservative average for
V_{ud}
(
15
)).

As illustrated in section
2
, the analysis
of the latest determinations of
V_{us}
obtained from kaon decays
and of
V_{ud}
from beta decays results in
a deviation from unitarity of the first row of the CKM matrix.
The SM unitarity relation (
8
) can be modified into
the relation in eq. (
443
)
if an extra vector-like up-type quark
u_{L4},u_{R4}
participates in the mixing
of the SM families. A quite large mixing with the first
family,
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|\approx|V_{L4u}|\approx 0.038(5)
(
444
),
is needed in order to explain the data,
see eq. (
444
)
(see also table
2
,
where in this scenario
\delta_{\text{CKM}}=|V_{L4u}|^{2}
).
In this way, the values of
V_{us}
obtained from the three determinations can be shifted
as shown in figure
5
,
where
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|\approx|V_{L4u}|
plays the same role as
|V_{ub^{\prime}}|
.
Then, we need to verify if such large mixing is compatible with experimental constraints from flavour changing decays and electroweak observables.

In table
7
constraints obtained in this section on the mixing of the new up-type vector-like quark
with the SM families are summarized
(
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|\approx|V_{L4u}|=0.03
is used as a conservative benchmark value).

In figures
18
and
19

the allowed area in the parameter space of
|V_{t^{\prime}s}|
and
|V_{L4c}|

is shown, using
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|=0.03
and
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|=0.04
respectively.

The blue area is excluded by constraints on neutral K-mesons system, as
obtained in section
5.2.2
and summarized in figure
16
. In figures
18
,
19
we use
the result:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}|<1.9\times 10^{-2}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{L4u}|}\right]

(540)

obtained for
M_{t^{\prime}}=1
TeV,
with the most convenient choice of the phase
\delta_{ds}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})=-0.04
.
The blue line indicates where the forbidden region expand
if the mass of the extra quark is taken as
M_{t^{\prime}}=5
TeV and
M_{t^{\prime}}=2.5
TeV
in the two figures respectively.
In fact,
for specific values of the phase
\delta_{ds}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d})
,
the upper limit is basically due to the restriction (
523
):

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|^{2}S_{0}(x_{t^{\prime}})<|V_{cs}^{*}V_{cd}|^{2}S_{0}(x_{c})

(541)

which gives:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|<5.2\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{t^{\prime}}}\right]\;,\qquad\quad|V_{t^{\prime}s}|<1.7\cdot 10^{-2}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{L4u}|}\right]\left[\frac{1\,\text{TeV}}{M_{t^{\prime}}}\right]

(542)

The red area is excluded by the constraints on

|V_{L4c}|
from
D^{0}
systems.
The new contribution to the mass difference is allowed to account for
the experimental limit on
\Delta m_{D\,\text{exp}}
at
95\%
C.L., which means that
the extra singlet quark can be thought as a way to explain the experimental result.
Then, as reported in eq. (
518
), the constraint is:

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}^{*}V_{L4c}|<1.5\times 10^{-4}\left[\frac{f(1\,\text{TeV})}{f(M_{t^{\prime}})}\right]^{1/2}\;,\qquad|V_{L4c}|<5.0\cdot 10^{-3}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{L4u}|}\right]

(543)

where
f(M_{t^{\prime}})
is defined in eq. (
513
).
The red line shows where the boundary is shifted
if the mass of the extra quark is taken as
M_{t^{\prime}}=5
TeV and
M_{t^{\prime}}=2.5
TeV
in the two cases respectively.
We can also notice that,
taking into account the relations (
470
), (
472
):

\displaystyle V_{L4u}\approx-\frac{h_{u1}^{*}v_{w}}{M_{t^{\prime}}}\;,\qquad\quad V_{L4c}\approx-\frac{h_{u2}^{*}v_{w}}{M_{t^{\prime}}}

(544)

where
h_{di}
are the Yukawa couplings defined in eq. (
394
),
from eq. (
543
) follows that the coupling of the 4th species with the 2nd family
h_{d2}

should be at least six times smaller
than the coupling with the first one
h_{d1}
.

From eq. (
488
) we have:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}|

\displaystyle\approx|V_{t^{\prime}d}V_{us}-V_{L4c}V_{cs}+V_{t^{\prime}b}V_{ts}|\approx

\displaystyle\approx\left||V_{t^{\prime}d}|V_{us}+e^{i\delta^{u}_{21}}|V_{L4c}|V_{cs}-e^{-i\delta_{db}}|V_{t^{\prime}b}||V_{ts}|\right|

(545)

where we use the parameterization in eqs. (
470
) and (
5
), and

\delta_{d}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}d})\approx\delta^{u}_{L1}
,

-\delta_{db}=\arg(V_{t^{\prime}b}V_{t^{\prime}d}^{*})\approx\delta^{u}_{L31}=\delta^{u}_{L3}-\delta^{u}_{L1}
,
and we can choose
\delta^{u}_{L1}=0
without loss of generality.
The green region is not allowed by
the relation (
5.4
) for any value of the relative phases,
using the values for
V_{t^{\prime}b}
allowed by the limit from the B-mesons system
in eq. (
532
), figure
17
:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}d}|<0.4\div 2.6\times 10^{-3}\;,\qquad|V_{t^{\prime}b}|<(1.3\div 8.5)\times 10^{-2}\left[\frac{0.03}{|V_{t^{\prime}d}|}\right]

(546)

Then, as regards the fourth row of the extended CKM matrix in eq. (
5
),
with
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|=0.03
and
M_{t^{\prime}}=1\,\text{TeV}
, we get:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}|<1.5\cdot 10^{-2}

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}b}|<8.5\cdot 10^{-2}

(547)

These conditions also satisfy the constraint on the product
V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}s}

(
533
) for the relative phase
needed in order to have the upper bounds (
547
),

\delta_{sb}=\text{Arg}(V_{t^{\prime}b}^{*}V_{t^{\prime}s})=\delta_{db}-\delta_{ds}\approx 2.77
.

However,
there is no much room to accommodate the relation in eq. (
5.4
)
without contradicting experimental constraints from neutral mesons mixing.
In particular, as shown in figure
18
, for
|V_{L4u}|=0.03
the mass of the extra quark
cannot exceed
\sim 5
TeV in order to leave allowed values in the parameter space.
If
M_{t^{\prime}}\approx 5
TeV, it should be that:

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}s}|\sim 3.8\cdot 10^{-3}

\displaystyle|V_{t^{\prime}b}|\sim 5.7\cdot 10^{-3}

(548)

which also satisfies the constraint (
533
).
This also means that in this case the mixing of

t^{\prime}
with the second and third families is respectively eight times and five times smaller
than the mixing with the d-quark.
Moreover
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|\simeq 0.03
is comparable to
|V_{cb}|

and ten times larger than
|V_{ub}|
.
This situation
may seem unnatural, but it cannot be excluded.

If
|V_{L4u}|\simeq 0.04
is needed in the unitarity relation (
443
),
then the constraints are more stringent,
and the available parameter space is remarkably reduced.
As can be inferred from figure
19
, in this case the mass of the extra quark
should not exceed
\sim 2.5
TeV.
Moreover, the constraint from K-mesons mixing in eq. (
542
) requires a specific
value for the phase
\delta_{ds}
in order to avoid constraints from CP violation.
In any case, it should be

M_{t^{\prime}}<5.8\,\text{TeV}
with
|V_{t^{\prime}d}|=0.03
and
M_{t^{\prime}}<4.4\,\text{TeV}
with

|V_{t^{\prime}d}|=0.04
for the perturbativity (
|h_{u\,1}|<1
).

In addition to that, the result for
Z
-boson decay rate into hadrons implies that
(
539
):

\displaystyle|V_{L4u}|^{2}<2.0\cdot 10^{-3}\;,\qquad\quad|V_{L4u}|<0.044

(549)

which can be in the range of values needed to solve the CKM unitarity problem
(for example, at
95\%
C.L.
|V_{L4u}|=0.038^{+0.008}_{-0.011}
(
163
)
using our conservative average for
V_{ud}
(
15
)).

6
Extra weak isodoublet

Weak isosinglets can be investigated in order to solve the problem of lack of unitarity
in
V_{\text{CKM}}

when
V_{ud}
is extracted from superallowed beta decays and
V_{us}
from kaon decays.
However, also between the results from kaon physics there is inconsistency.
In particular, it can be noticed that the lack of compatibility lies between vector and and axial-vector couplings in weak charged currents.
In fact, determination
B
(
19
) is extracted from the axial-vector coupling of leptonic kaon decays
(as also determination
|V_{us}|=0.22567(42)

from
K\mu 2
decays
Martinelli
,
which agrees with determination
B
),
while determination
A
(
11
) results from the vector coupling of semileptonic kaon decays.
Besides, determination
C
(
20
) is obtained from the vector coupling of beta decays.
As displayed in Fig
2
,
determination
A
and
C
are deviated from the axial-vector determination in opposite directions.
In fact, there is
2.7\sigma
discrepancy between determination
B
(
19
) and
C
(
20
)
and
3\sigma
discrepancy between determinations
A
(
11
) and
B
.
As it will be shown, the insertion of an extra vector-like isodoublet generates weak right
currents which modify vector and axial-vector couplings thus offering the possibility to explain both the gaps.
However, also in this case FCNC emerge at tree level and
predictions for electroweak processes are modified, so experimental limits
must be checked.

Let us introduce the additional vector-like
SU(2)
-doublet family:

\displaystyle q_{L4}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L4}\\
d_{L4}\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad q_{R4}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{R4}\\
d_{R4}\end{array}\right)

New Yukawa couplings and mass terms should appear in the Lagrangian:

y^{u\prime}_{ij}\tilde{\varphi}\overline{q_{Li}}^{\prime}u_{Rj}+y^{d\prime}_{ij}\varphi\overline{q_{Li}}^{\prime}d_{Rj}+m_{i}\overline{q_{Li}}^{\prime}q_{R4}+\text{h.c.}

with
i=1,2,3,4
,
j=1,2,3
.
As regards the mass terms

m_{i}\overline{q^{\prime}_{Li}}q_{R4}+\text{h.c.}
,
since the four species of right-handed singlets
q_{Li}^{\prime}
have identical quantum numbers,
a unitary transformation can be applied on the four components
q_{Li}^{\prime}
so that

m_{i}=0
for
i=1,2,3
.
Then the Yukawa couplings and the extra mass term are:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left[y_{ij}^{u}\tilde{\varphi}\overline{q_{Li}}u_{Rj}+y_{ij}^{d}\varphi\overline{q_{Li}}d_{Rj}\right]+M_{4q}\overline{q_{L4}}q_{R4}+\text{h.c.}

(554)

The down quark mass matrix looks like:

\displaystyle\overline{d_{Li}}\mathbf{m}^{(d)}_{ij}d_{Rj}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\overline{d_{L1}}&\overline{d_{L2}}&\overline{d_{L3}}&\overline{d_{L4}}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}&&&0\\
&\mathbf{y}^{(d)}_{3\times 3}v_{w}&&0\\
&&&0\\
y^{d}_{41}v_{w}&y^{d}_{42}v_{w}&y^{d}_{43}v_{w}&M_{4q}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{R1}\\
d_{R2}\\
d_{R3}\\
d_{R4}\end{array}\right)+\text{h.c.}

where
v_{w}=174
GeV and

\mathbf{y}^{(d)}_{3\times 3}
is the
3\times 3
matrix of Yukawa couplings, and similarly for the up-type quarks.
The mass matrices can be diagonalized with positive eigenvalues by biunitary transformations:

\displaystyle V_{L}^{(d)\dagger}\mathbf{m}^{(d)}V_{R}^{(d)}=\mathbf{m}^{(d)}_{\text{diag}}=\text{diag}(y_{d}v_{w}\,,y_{s}v_{w}\,,y_{b}v_{w}\,,M_{q})

\displaystyle V_{L}^{(u)\dagger}\mathbf{m}^{(u)}V_{R}^{(u)}=\mathbf{m}^{(u)}_{\text{diag}}=\text{diag}(y_{u}v_{w}\,,y_{c}v_{w}\,,y_{t}v_{w}\,,M_{q})

(564)

\mathbf{m}^{(d,u)}_{\text{diag}}
are the diagonal matrices of mass eigenvalues

m_{d,s,b}=y_{d,s,b}v_{w}
and
m_{u,c,t}=y_{u,c,t}v_{w}
and
M_{q}\approx M_{4q}
.

V^{(d,u)}_{L,R}
are unitary
4\times 4
matrices:

\displaystyle V^{(d)}_{R}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{R\,1d}&V_{R\,1s}&V_{R\,1b}&V_{R\,1b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,2d}&V_{R\,2s}&V_{R\,2b}&V_{R\,2b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,3d}&V_{R\,3s}&V_{R\,3b}&V_{R\,3b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,4d}&V_{R\,4s}&V_{R\,4b}&V_{R\,4b^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)\,,

\displaystyle V^{(u)}_{R}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{R\,1u}&V_{R\,1c}&V_{R\,1t}&V_{R\,1t^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,2u}&V_{R\,2c}&V_{R\,2t}&V_{R\,2t^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,3u}&V_{R\,3c}&V_{R\,3t}&V_{R\,3t^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,4u}&V_{R\,4c}&V_{R\,4t}&V_{R\,4t^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)

and analogously for the matrices diagonalizing from the left.
Weak eigenstates in terms of mass eigenstates are:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{1}\\
d_{2}\\
d_{3}\\
d_{4}\end{array}\right)_{L,R}=V^{(d)}_{L,R}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d\\
s\\
b\\
b^{\prime}\end{array}\right)_{L,R}\,,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{1}\\
u_{2}\\
u_{3}\\
u_{4}\end{array}\right)_{L,R}=V^{(u)}_{L,R}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
c\\
t\\
t^{\prime}\end{array}\right)_{L,R}

The charged-current Lagrangian is changed in:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{cc}=

\displaystyle\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{4}(\overline{u_{Li}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{Li})W_{\mu}^{+}+\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\overline{u_{4R}}\gamma^{\mu}d_{4R}W_{\mu}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\overline{u_{L}}&\overline{c_{L}}&\overline{t_{L}}&\overline{t_{L}^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)\gamma^{\mu}V_{\text{CKM,L}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{L}\\
s_{L}\\
b_{L}\\
b_{L}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)W_{\mu}^{+}+

\displaystyle+\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\overline{u_{R}}&\overline{c_{R}}&\overline{t_{R}}&\overline{t_{R}^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)\gamma^{\mu}V_{\text{CKM,R}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{R}\\
s_{R}\\
b_{R}\\
b_{R}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)W_{\mu}^{+}+\text{h.c.}

where
V_{\text{CKM,L}}=V_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}V_{L}^{(d)}
is a
4\times 4
unitary matrix:

\displaystyle V_{\text{CKM,L}}^{\dagger}V_{\text{CKM,L}}=V_{\text{CKM,L}}V_{\text{CKM,L}}^{\dagger}=\mathbf{1}

(599)

\displaystyle V_{\text{CKM,L}}=V_{L}^{(u){\dagger}}V_{L}^{(d)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{L\,ud}&V_{L\,us}&V_{L\,ub}&V_{L\,ub^{\prime}}\\
V_{L\,cd}&V_{L\,cs}&V_{L\,cb}&V_{L\,cb^{\prime}}\\
V_{L\,td}&V_{L\,ts}&V_{L\,tb}&V_{L\,tb^{\prime}}\\
V_{L\,t^{\prime}d}&V_{L\,t^{\prime}s}&V_{L\,t^{\prime}b}&V_{L\,t^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)

In addition to that,
in this case, as shown in eq. (
6
),
the charged current Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{cc}
also involves non-diagonal
right weak charged currents
originated by the mixing of the vector-like family with the SM families,
with mixing matrix
V_{\text{CKM,R}}
:

\displaystyle V_{\text{CKM,R}}

\displaystyle=V_{R}^{(u){\dagger}}\text{diag}(0,0,0,1)V_{R}^{(d)}=

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{R\,4u}^{*}V_{R\,4d}&V_{R\,4u}^{*}V_{R\,4s}&V_{R\,4u}^{*}V_{R\,4b}&V_{R\,4u}^{*}V_{R\,4b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,4c}^{*}V_{R\,4d}&V_{R\,4c}^{*}V_{R\,4s}&V_{R\,4c}^{*}V_{R\,4b}&V_{R\,4c}^{*}V_{R\,4b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,4t}^{*}V_{R\,4d}&V_{R\,4t}^{*}V_{R\,4s}&V_{R\,4t}^{*}V_{R\,4b}&V_{R\,4t}^{*}V_{R\,4b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{R\,4d}&V_{R\,4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{R\,4s}&V_{R\,4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{R\,4b}&V_{R\,4t^{\prime}}^{*}V_{R\,4b^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)=

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{R\,ud}&V_{R\,us}&V_{R\,ub}&V_{R\,ub^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,cd}&V_{R\,cs}&V_{R\,cb}&V_{R\,cb^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,td}&V_{R\,ts}&V_{R\,tb}&V_{R\,tb^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,t^{\prime}d}&V_{R\,t^{\prime}s}&V_{R\,t^{\prime}b}&V_{R\,t^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)

where

V_{R4\alpha}
are the elements of the fourth row of the
matrices
V_{R}^{(d)}
,
V_{R}^{(u)}
in eq. (
6
),
and we defined the elements
V_{R\alpha\beta}
.
Clearly,
V_{\text{CKM,R}}
is not unitary.

Regarding the LH particles, the matrices
V^{(d)}_{L}
,
V^{(u)}_{L}
can be parameterized with the same parameterization as in eq. (
4
).
However,
in this scenario mixings in the left-handed sector are much smaller and indeed negligible:

\displaystyle\tilde{s}^{u,d}_{Li}\approx-\frac{y^{u,d}_{i}y^{u,d*}_{4i}v^{2}_{w}}{M^{2}_{q}}

(612)

where
y^{d}_{i}=y_{d,s,b}
,
y^{u}_{i}=y_{u,c,t}
, and

V^{(u,d)}_{Lij}y^{u,d}_{4j}\approx y^{u,d}_{4i}
.
As regards the right-handed sector, without loss of generality, the basis can be chosen in which
the mixing between the SM three families has been diagonalized:

\displaystyle V^{(d)}_{R}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}V_{R\,1d}&V_{R\,1s}&V_{R\,1b}&V_{R\,1b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,2d}&V_{R\,2s}&V_{R\,2b}&V_{R\,2b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,3d}&V_{R\,3s}&V_{R\,3b}&V_{R\,3b^{\prime}}\\
V_{R\,4d}&V_{R\,4s}&V_{R\,4b}&V_{R\,4b^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}c_{R1}^{d}&0&0&-\tilde{s}^{d}_{R1}\\
-\tilde{s}^{d}_{R2}\tilde{s}^{d*}_{R1}&c^{d}_{R2}&0&-\tilde{s}^{d}_{R2}\\
-\tilde{s}^{d}_{R3}\tilde{s}^{d*}_{R1}&-\tilde{s}^{d*}_{R2}\tilde{s}^{d}_{R3}&c^{d}_{R3}&-\tilde{s}^{d}_{R3}\\
\tilde{s}^{d*}_{R1}&\tilde{s}^{d*}_{R2}&\tilde{s}^{*d}_{R3}&c_{R1}^{d}c^{d}_{R2}c^{d}_{R3}\end{array}\right)

where
c^{d}_{Ri}
are cosines and

\tilde{s}^{d}_{Ri}
are complex sines of angles in the

1\,4
,
2\,4
,
3\,4
family
planes parameterizing the mixing of the first three families
with the vector-like doublet:

\displaystyle\tilde{s}^{d}_{Ri}=\sin\theta^{d}_{Ri4}e^{i\delta^{d}_{Ri}}=s^{d}_{Ri}e^{i\delta^{d}_{Ri}}\;,\qquad\quad c^{d}_{Ri}=\cos\theta^{d}_{Ri4}

\displaystyle\delta^{d}_{Rij}=\delta^{d}_{Ri}-\delta^{d}_{Rj}

(621)

and similarly for
V_{R}^{(u)}
.
As it can be seen by comparing the mass matrix in eq. (
6
) with the matrices in eqs.
(
4
) and (
5
),
in this case the
angles parameterizing the mixing of the first three families with the fourth family
in the right-handed sector are analogous in magnitude to the ones of the left-handed sector in the previous sections:

\displaystyle\tilde{s}^{u,d}_{Ri}\approx-\frac{y^{u,d*}_{4i}v_{w}}{M_{q}}

(622)

at order
O(|y^{u,d}_{4i}|(y^{d}_{i})^{2}\frac{v_{w}^{3}}{M_{b^{\prime}}^{3}}+|y^{u,d}_{4j}|^{2}|y^{u,d}_{4i}|\frac{v_{w}^{3}}{M_{b^{\prime}}^{3}})
,
i,j=1,2,3
,
y^{d}_{i}=y_{d,s,b}
,
y^{u}_{i}=y_{u,c,t}
.
The elements of the last row (and also of the last column) in
V^{(d)}_{R}
and
V^{(u)}_{R}

correspond to the mixing angles of the SM families with the vector-like one:

\displaystyle V_{R4d}\approx\tilde{s}_{R1}^{d*}\,,

\displaystyle V_{R4s}\approx\tilde{s}_{R2}^{d*}\,,

\displaystyle V_{R4b}=\tilde{s}_{R3}^{d*}

\displaystyle V_{R4u}\approx\tilde{s}_{R1}^{u*}\,,

\displaystyle V_{R4c}\approx\tilde{s}_{R2}^{u*}\,,

\displaystyle V_{R4t}=\tilde{s}_{R3}^{u*}

(623)

Let us focus on
the piece of the charged current Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{cc}
in eq. (
6
)
determining the couplings of
u
-quark with down type quarks
(which in the SM would correspond with the determination of the first row of CKM matrix):

\displaystyle\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}V_{L\,ud}d_{L}+\overline{u_{R}}V_{R\,ud}\gamma^{\mu}d_{R})\,W_{\mu}+

\displaystyle+\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}(\overline{u_{L}}\gamma^{\mu}V_{L\,us}s_{L}+\overline{u_{R}}V_{R\,us}\gamma^{\mu}s_{R})\,W_{\mu}+\text{h.c.}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{V}_{ud}\>\overline{u}\gamma^{\mu}\left(1-\gamma^{5}k_{A}^{ud}\right)d\>W_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{g}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{V}_{us}\>\overline{u}\gamma^{\mu}\left(1-\gamma^{5}k_{A}^{us}\right)s\>W_{\mu}

(624)

where, from eq. (
6
):

\displaystyle V_{Rud}=V_{R\,4u}^{*}V_{R\,4d}\>,\quad\qquad V_{Rus}=V_{R\,4u}^{*}V_{R\,4s}

(625)

and similarly for the mixing with the bottom quark.
The vector and axial-vector couplings are respectively:

\hat{V}_{u\alpha}=V_{L\,u\alpha}+V_{R\,u\alpha}

and
k_{A}^{u\alpha}\hat{V}_{u\alpha}=V_{L\,u\alpha}-V_{R\,u\alpha}
,
with
\alpha=d,s,b
.
The most precise determination of SM
V_{ud}
comes from superallowed beta decays.
Superallowed
0^{+}
-
0^{+}
beta decays
are Fermi transitions, that is they uniquely depend on the vector part of the hadronic weak interaction

G_{V}=G_{F}V_{ud}
.
This means that the determination of the weak coupling in superallowed beta decays gives

|\hat{V}_{ud}|
appearing in (
624
).
Regarding
|V_{us}|
, it is determined both from semileptonic kaon decays (
K\ell 3
)
and from the ratio of leptonic kaon decays (
K\mu 2
) and leptonic pion decays.
It is assumed that only the vector current contributes to semileptonic kaon decays, that is
the coupling is given by
\hat{V}_{us}
in (
624
).
Leptonic decays instead depend on the axial-vector coupling, which corresponds to

k_{A}^{us}\hat{V}_{us}
in (
624
).
Then, in this scenario,
the determinations of
V_{us}
obtained from eqs. (
11
), (
13
), and (
15
) correspond to the following observables:

\displaystyle A\,:\qquad|V_{L\,us}+V_{R\,us}|=0.22326(55)

(626)

\displaystyle B\,:\qquad\frac{|V_{L\,us}-V_{R\,us}|}{|V_{L\,ud}-V_{R\,ud}|}=0.23130(49)

(627)

\displaystyle C\,:\qquad|V_{L\,ud}+V_{R\,ud}|=0.97376(16)

(628)

As regards
V_{\text{CKM,L}}
,
as already stated (
599
), it is a unitary matrix, so for the first row it holds that:

\displaystyle|V_{L\,ud}|^{2}+|V_{L\,us}|^{2}+|V_{L\,ub}|^{2}=1-|V_{L\,ub^{\prime}}|^{2}\approx 1

(629)

In fact,

V_{\text{CKM,L}}
can be parameterized as:

\displaystyle V_{\text{CKM,L}}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}c_{L1}^{u}&0&0&\tilde{s}^{u}_{L1}\\
0&c^{u}_{L2}&0&\tilde{s}^{u}_{L2}\\
0&0&c^{u}_{L3}&\tilde{s}^{u}_{L3}\\
-\tilde{s}^{u*}_{L1}&-\tilde{s}^{u*}_{L2}&-\tilde{s}^{u*}_{L3}&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{-1\tabcolsep}} c@{\hspace{-1\tabcolsep}} cc}\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&&0\\
\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&V_{\text{CKM}\,3\times 3}\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&&0\\
\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&&0\\
0\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&0\hfil\hskip-6.0pt&0&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}c_{L1}^{d}&0&0&-\tilde{s}^{d}_{L1}\\
0&c^{d}_{L2}&0&-\tilde{s}^{d}_{L2}\\
0&0&c^{d}_{L3}&-\tilde{s}^{d}_{L3}\\
\tilde{s}^{d*}_{L1}&\tilde{s}^{d*}_{L2}&\tilde{s}^{*d}_{L3}&1\end{array}\right)

where

V_{\text{CKM}\,3\times 3}
is basically the same as the
3\times 3
submatrix of

V_{\text{CKM,L}}
and contains
3
angles and one phase.
The complex sines
\tilde{s}^{u,d}_{Li}
are defined in eq. (
612
) (and
also the phases in
\tilde{s}^{u,d}_{L1}
and
\tilde{s}^{u,d}_{L2}

can be absorbed in quark fields).
Then,

V_{L\,ub^{\prime}}\sim-\tilde{s}^{d}_{L1}+\tilde{s}^{u}_{L1}\sim O(y^{u,d}_{1}y^{u,d}_{41}v_{w}^{2}/M_{q}^{2})
is totally negligible.

|V_{L\,ub}|
has almost no influence
and the value of
|V_{ub}|
as exctracted in the SM can be used.
Hence,
|V_{L\,ud}|
can be determined from
|V_{L\,us}|
by using the unitarity relation (
629
).
Then, the system of the three different determinations A, B, C
is exactly solved by three real parameters
V_{Lus}
,
V_{Rud}
,
V_{Rus}
.
Using
the dataset (
626
), (
627
), (
628
),
the solution gives:

\displaystyle V_{R\,us}=V_{R4u}^{*}V_{R4s}=-1.16(37)\cdot 10^{-3}\,,

\displaystyle V_{R\,ud}=V_{R4u}^{*}V_{R4d}=-0.73(18)\cdot 10^{-3}

(642)

with
V_{Lus}=0.22441(35)
and
V_{Lud}=0.97449(8)
.
Figure
20
basically shows the interpretation of the
determinations of
V_{us}
obtained from the three different processes
in the scenario with the extra isodoublet of quarks, as given in eqs. (
626
), (
627
), (
628
).
The element of the unitary matrix
V_{\text{CKM,L}}

V_{Lus}
lies in between determinations A and B, and
the gap among the two is explained by the splitting due to
\pm V_{Rus}
(the shift of determination
B is dominated by
V_{Rus}
).
On the other hand,
V_{Rud}
explains the apparent lack of unitarity. In fact,
the values of
V_{Lus}
and
V_{Lud}
are linked by the unitarity of
V_{\text{CKM,L}}
,
but the vector coupling giving determination C includes the additional coupling
V_{Rud}
.

By substituting determination C with the determinations

C_{1}
or
C_{2}
(see table
1
),
the needed value of
V_{Rus}
remains almost the same.
Using determination
C_{1}
:

|V_{ud}|=0.97370(14)
with the dataset (
626
), (
627
), the
result would be

V_{Rus}=-1.16(37)\cdot 10^{-3}
,
V_{Rud}=-0.78(16)\cdot 10^{-3}
with

V_{Lus}=0.22442(35)
,
while using determination
C_{2}
:
|V_{ud}|=0.97389(18)
, the solution is

V_{Rus}=-1.14(37)\cdot 10^{-3}
,
V_{Rud}=-0.60(19)\cdot 10^{-3}
with

V_{Lus}=0.22442(35)
.

Figure 20:

Explanation of the anomalies of
V_{us}
determinations in the SM extension with an extra vector-like isodoublet
(b^{\prime},t^{\prime})
.
The three different determinations
V_{us}^{A}
,
V_{us}^{B}
and

in eqs. (

), (

), (

)
are obtained from the experimental results in eqs.

However, in presence of an extra isodoublet mixing with SM families, the three observables
would correspond to the couplings in eqs. (
